Runtime Docs Audit

Contents

  • Code defects the docs items depend on
  • 1. Bun guide
  • DOC-B1 The guide states the instrumentation model backwards and never names the plugin
  • DOC-B2 The Bun integrations table lists three integrations that are not installed and omits eleven that are
  • DOC-B3 Express, Koa, Hapi, Fastify and Next.js work on Bun and no page says so; Hono has an undocumented bundling caveat
  • DOC-B4 Guide hygiene: no Bun version requirement, `npm install` in the Hono guide, no breadcrumbs page
  • 2. Deno guide
  • DOC-D1 The guide never mentions the runtime hook or which instrumentation is automatic
  • DOC-D2 The Deno integrations table lists 8 auto-enabled integrations; the SDK installs 39
  • DOC-D3 The permission section is incomplete and the two commands are shown as alternatives
  • DOC-D4 Fastify and Hono work on Deno without the hook, Express, Koa and Hapi need it and crash with it, and no page says any of this
  • DOC-D5 The Deno v7-to-v8 migration include files are copies of the Bun ones
  • DOC-D6 The guide shows only the `npm:` specifier, never `deno add` or an import map, and events name a registry that does not ship the package
  • 3. Cloudflare guide
  • DOC-C1 Four places document the `@sentry/cloudflare/nodejs_compat` entrypoint that v11 removes
  • DOC-C2 `features/d1.mdx` documents a removed function and hides that D1 is automatic
  • DOC-C3 `enableRpcTracePropagation` appears in three places and has no options entry; v11 replaces it
  • DOC-C4 The Vite plugin page documents `_experimental` options that no longer exist and says the plugin is a no-op by default
  • DOC-C5 `wrapRequestHandler` is imported from the main entry, and the `honoIntegration` page survives its removal
  • DOC-C6 No page lists which bindings produce spans, and the list changed twice this year
  • DOC-C7 The frameworks section is missing React Router, says "Pages", and the TanStack Start page cannot render its wrangler step
  • DOC-C8 Snippet hygiene across the Cloudflare includes
  • 4. Cross-cutting
  • X1 "Tracing is enabled by default" on all three runtimes contradicts the guides
  • X2 There is no v10-to-v11 page, and `MIGRATION.md` does not say how to enable channel instrumentation per runtime
  • X3 There is no runtime-by-framework or runtime-by-integration matrix, and every table is hand-maintained

Runtime docs audit: Bun, Deno, Cloudflare

What the Bun, Deno and Cloudflare guides in getsentry/sentry-docs say, checked claim by claim against the shipped @sentry/bun, @sentry/deno and @sentry/cloudflare packages, with @sentry/node as the baseline. It also answers which frameworks work on each runtime and whether the docs say so. This is the DOC7 item the prior audit (https://gist.github.com/isaacs/2560b72b22a7007bc8098bf33c83020d) left open; its items are cited as gist DOC1 and so on and not repeated unless a repro changed their state.

SDK repo~/dev/sentry-javascript, audited at 6f2da7483d (develop, v11 line, package version 10.67.0) in a detached worktree at ~/dev/sentry-worktrees/audit-runtime-docs
Docs repo~/dev/sentry-docs at 046ce5942 (2026-09-01)
Published@sentry/{bun,deno,cloudflare,node} 10.73.0 on npm. The docs describe v10; develop already carries the v11 breaking changes, and MIGRATION.md lists them.
Runtimesnode v26.5.0, bun 1.4.0, deno 2.9.0 and 2.8.3 (the CI pin), wrangler 4.56.0 to 4.129.0
Datesections 1 to 4 on 2026-09-02 with ticket states read the same day, section 5 on 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-07. A batch of audit issues from the gist author landed in sentry-javascript that day (#23881 to #23899).

Nothing was committed. Every behavior claim was run against the built packages; the probes are in probes/, their outputs in probes/results/, and probes/rerun.sh plus probes/FW-run.sh re-run all of them. The proposed quick starts in quickstart/ were built as fresh apps in qs/ and run on the develop build and on the published 10.73.0; quickstart/VERIFICATION.md maps every guide claim to a result. The main checkout was fast-forwarded twice during the audit, so the packages were built in the pinned worktree.

[proven] a probe confirms the claim. [read] the claim rests on reading (docs wording, CI config). P0 a page that loses data for a common setup. P1 a page that is wrong or missing for something users will hit. P2 consistency and hygiene.

Coverage of the 21 items in sections 1 to 4, plus the 4 checked items:

TagMeaningCount

Issue proposals

Every item below carries a proposed GitHub issue for getsentry/sentry-docs, a title and a description. The proposals describe only wiring and behavior verified to work on the develop line today, name the page to change, and state verified limits as plain facts. They ask for no SDK changes and contain no workarounds that rely on internal paths or hand-written copies of SDK code.

Snapshot check on 2026-09-07

Docs master moved to b568ebbd3 (2026-09-04) after the audit snapshot 046ce5942; none of the 70 commits touches the Bun, Deno, Cloudflare, Hono or Elysia guides or their platform includes, so every file and line citation below still holds. The SDK develop branch moved to d6426ad581 (2026-09-07); the only behavior change in the audited packages is the Deno stream-cancellation fix c619da370, which affects report finding F24 and no docs item here.

Second pass on 2026-09-08. Every item was re-verified by an independent read against docs master aebda0ee4 and develop 03aaf42455: line citations moved with the docs restructure and are refreshed below; the v10-to-v11 migration page (common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx) now exists and already carries several of the Cloudflare changes, which narrows X2, DOC-C1, DOC-C2, DOC-C4 and DOC-C5 to the product pages; DOC-C3 shrank to four missing options after 3acad630b; DOC-Q2 drops to P1 because the options file is documented since 9bfe0e96c; DOC-F49 rises to P1 because the TanStack Start page never names sentryTanstackStart(); the Bun database-span limits were overturned by the recorded refutations (spans work once the channel is pinned); and the browser guide joined the quick starts as DOC-Q7 to DOC-Q11. The per-item change logs are in _reports/verification/edits-*.json. A record-consistency check on 2026-09-09 found that eleven refutation records with a works-after-all verdict (Astro, Next.js, Nuxt, React Router and SolidStart on Bun; Elysia, Fastify and Koa on Deno; NestJS on Workers) ship startup logs only, so the items that cite them state the recorded result and the shipped directory cannot re-prove it; the list is in _reports/ERRATA.md.

Code defects the docs items depend on

These were found while checking the pages. They are not docs work, but several pages cannot be written correctly until they are decided. One line each; the probe outputs are referenced from the docs items.

Code itemWhat it isBlocksTicket

K2Deno fetch gets no span and no sentry-trace/baggage (probes/results/PROP-deno.json)DOC-D2#23891 (open)

Deno bug; a second load hook that sets format: 'json' for .json URLs works around it (deep probe X03), so an SDK-side shim in register.ts is feasibleDOC-D1, DOC-D4denoland/deno#36240 (open), fix denoland/deno#36243 (open); needs a sentry-javascript ticket

K11Bun plugin build: an instrumented module that first evaluates inside a request (mysql's lazily required lib/Connection.js, or a dynamic import() of a driver in a handler) never gets spans, on develop and 10.73.0, bun 1.4.0 and 1.3.14. Root cause, found by the deep probe (X02): Bun's node:diagnostics_channel garbage-collects a channel and its subscribers when nothing holds a strong reference, and the SDK drops the channel object it subscribes to (packages/server-utils/src/tracing-channel.ts:200-208). Fix: retain the channels. Workarounds: import drivers and create pools at module scope, or import "mysql/lib/Connection"DOC-B1, DOC-B3none, needs one (an SDK fix, plus a Bun report)
K12@sentry/deno/import only transforms packages under a local node_modules; from Deno's npm cache nothing is injected and nothing warns (qs/qs10/deno-cache)DOC-D1none, needs one

1. Bun guide

Pages: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx and guides/bun/configuration/integrations/contextlines.mdx. No config.yml, no features/, no frameworks/, no breadcrumbs page (the common page lists javascript.bun in notSupported and there is no guide override, unlike Deno).

DOC-B1The guide states the instrumentation model backwards and never names the pluginP0proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Rewrite the Bun guide's instrumentation step around @sentry/bun/plugin

Description

The Bun guide at guides/bun/index.mdx:88 and :100 tells the user to run bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js, and the alert at :90-94 states that Sentry's auto-instrumentation does not work with bundled code and that bundling requires manual instrumentation. @sentry/bun/plugin is not named anywhere on the page, and logs is used as an onboarding block at :182 but is missing from the options at :20-22.

What was observed on bun 1.4.0: a bundle built with Bun.build({ plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin records library and framework spans, while running the same app unbundled with bun run records none. packages/bun/src/plugin.ts:14 states the plugin is build-only. The unbundled diagnostic message is printed only with debug: true.

What to change:

  • Keep the --preload step, described as what guarantees init order.
  • Add a "Build with the Sentry plugin" step showing Bun.build({ entrypoints: [...], plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) imported from @sentry/bun/plugin.
  • Replace the alert at :90-94 with a plain statement: library and framework instrumentation requires a build with the plugin; bun run on unbundled source records manual spans plus Bun.serve, node:http and fetch spans.
  • Add logs to the onboarding options at :20-22, or remove the block at :182.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-B1

gist DOC1, re-run. guides/bun/index.mdx:90-94:

Sentry's auto-instrumentation does not work with bundled code, including Bun's single-file executables. [...] If you need to bundle your application, you'll need to manually instrument your code.

and :88, :100 tell the user to run bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js. On this commit the opposite holds: only a bundle built with @sentry/bun/plugin records library and framework spans, and bun run records none (K1). packages/bun/src/plugin.ts:14: "This is BUILD-ONLY. Runtime instrumentation (bun run) is currently not supported." The plugin is not mentioned anywhere in the guide. logs is referenced as an onboarding block at :182 but not offered at :20-22.

A user who follows the page gets the full default integration set (42 at 6f2da7483d, 43 on develop head), no database, AI or framework spans, and no visible message.

Repro probes/B-PLUGIN-build.mjs + probes/B-PLUGIN-app.mjs, bun 1.4.0, verdict: confirmed. probes/results/B-PLUGIN.txt:

build ok: true []
-- bundled output run:   [Sentry] Bundler plugin ran, injected libraries=["express"]   {"orchestrion":{"bundler":["express"]}}
-- same app unbundled:   [Sentry] No diagnostics-channel injection detected. ...       {"orchestrion":{}}

The unbundled warning only prints with debug: true (probes/results/INIT-bun.json). The getting-started reproduction (DOC-Q4, bun 1.4.2) reaches the same result on the documented --preload path.

Test coverage the bun-mysql e2e app (dev-packages/e2e-tests/test-applications/bun-mysql/build.ts:6-21) pins the plugin path. Nothing pins the --preload path the guide recommends.

Work item. Rewrite "Apply Instrumentation to Your App": keep --preload for init order, add a "Build with the Sentry plugin" step with Bun.build({ plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }), and replace the alert at :90-94 with a plain statement that bun run on unbundled source records manual spans plus the Bun.serve, node:http and fetch spans (see DOC-B3 for what that means per framework). Add logs to the onboarding buttons or remove the block. Land with K1 so the page and the warning agree.

Prior art _(related history only)_. sentry-docs#15539 (closed 2025-11) put the --preload advice on the page via sentry-docs#15575, before the plugin existed. sentry-docs#16843 (merged 2026-03) reframed the AI pages as "manual on Bun" without touching the Bun guide. No issue names the bundling paragraph or @sentry/bun/plugin. #14202 (open since 2024-11) is a user asking exactly this question.

DOC-B2The Bun integrations table lists three integrations that are not installed and omits twelve that areP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Correct the Bun integrations table in platform-includes/.../javascript.bun.mdx

Description

platform-includes/configuration/integrations/javascript.bun.mdx:5-37 lists dedupeIntegration (:9), nativeNodeFetchIntegration (:24) and inboundFiltersIntegration (:14) as auto-enabled, and pinoIntegration at :47, while fsIntegration is shown for Bun with no row.

What was observed: the Bun SDK installs 42 integrations at 6f2da7483d, and 43 on develop head, where mastraIntegration() joined getTracingIntegrations(). It ships fetchIntegration rather than nativeNodeFetchIntegration, installs inbound filtering as EventFilters, and does not install dedupeIntegration. Integrations installed with no row in the table: fetchIntegration, bunHttpServerIntegration, processSessionIntegration, expressIntegration, fastifyIntegration, hapiIntegration, koaIntegration, awsIntegration, langGraphIntegration, postgresJsIntegration, spanStreamingIntegration and mastraIntegration. The list is produced by getDefaultIntegrations() in packages/bun/src/sdk.ts:77-79, which spreads getDefaultIntegrationsWithoutPerformance() at :47-74, plus packages/server-utils/src/integrations/index.ts:30-62 (:30-63 on develop head). spanStreamingIntegration is not part of that list: the client adds it in packages/core/src/server-runtime-client.ts:56, so a table generated from getDefaultIntegrations() alone would still miss it. The channel-based integrations only produce spans after a Bun.build with @sentry/bun/plugin.

What to change:

  • Drop the dedupeIntegration row; rename nativeNodeFetchIntegration to fetchIntegration; rename inboundFiltersIntegration to the installed eventFiltersIntegration.
  • Add rows for the twelve installed integrations listed above.
  • Add a "Requires @sentry/bun/plugin" column and tick it for the channel-based rows.
  • Fix the gating so common/configuration/integrations/pino.mdx is shown for Bun, and give fsIntegration a row or hide it.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-B2

platform-includes/configuration/integrations/javascript.bun.mdx:5-37 against probes/results/INIT-bun.json (42 installed at 6f2da7483d; 43 on develop head, where mastraIntegration() was added to getTracingIntegrations()):

  • State which instrumentation works without the hook.

X1"Tracing is enabled by default" on all three runtimes contradicts the guidesP2read

Proposed issue docs-change

Fix "Tracing is enabled by default" in the Bun, Deno and Cloudflare enable-tracing includes

Description

platform-includes/performance/enable-tracing/javascript.bun.mdx:1, javascript.deno.mdx:1 and javascript.cloudflare.mdx:1 each read "Tracing is enabled by default in the Sentry <Runtime> SDK." The guides for those runtimes, and platform-includes/performance/enable-automatic-instrumentation/javascript.bun.mdx:1 and javascript.cloudflare.mdx:1, tell the user to set tracesSampleRate or tracesSampler.

What was observed: hasSpansEnabled in packages/core/src/utils/hasSpansEnabled.ts:23-36 records spans only when tracesSampleRate or tracesSampler is set, on every runtime. On Bun there is a second gate on top of it: getPerformanceIntegrations in packages/bun/src/sdk.ts:38-44 returns no performance integrations while hasSpansEnabled is false. The Deno and Cloudflare SDKs have no such gate and always install the tracing integrations, so the general reason the sentence is wrong is the sampling option, not the integration set.

What to change:

  • Replace the first sentence in all three includes with a statement that tracing is enabled by setting tracesSampleRate or tracesSampler in Sentry.init, and that no spans are recorded when neither is set.
  • Keep the wording consistent with the enable-automatic-instrumentation includes for Bun and Cloudflare, which already require the option. There is no Deno variant of that include, so Deno falls back to javascript.mdx.

Source: runtime docs audit, item X1

platform-includes/performance/enable-tracing/javascript.{bun,deno,cloudflare}.mdx:1 each read "Tracing is enabled by default in the Sentry <Runtime> SDK." The guides and platform-includes/performance/enable-automatic-instrumentation/javascript.{bun,cloudflare}.mdx:1 require tracesSampleRate or tracesSampler; hasSpansEnabled (packages/core/src/utils/hasSpansEnabled.ts:23-36) records spans only when one of the two is set, on all three runtimes. Bun adds an integration-level gate on top (getPerformanceIntegrations, packages/bun/src/sdk.ts:38-44); Deno (packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:28-49, which always spreads getTracingIntegrations()) and Cloudflare have none. There is no enable-automatic-instrumentation/javascript.deno.mdx, so the consistency ask covers Bun and Cloudflare only.

Work item. Replace the sentence in all three includes.

DOC-F1Hono on Bun: documented partialP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Document Hono on Bun: unbundled run and the Bun.build plugin variant

Description

The Hono guide names Bun at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hono/index.mdx:12, has a Bun install tab at hono/index.mdx:84-86 and a Bun setup snippet at hono/index.mdx:278-300, but it never shows how to start the app or what the runtime delivers. The Bun guide teaches only bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js (bun/index.mdx:100) and carries a "Bundled Code Limitation" alert at bun/index.mdx:90-94. sentryBunPlugin, @sentry/bun/plugin and Bun.build appear nowhere in the docs.

Verified on Hono 4.13.x with @sentry/hono/bun 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0 and re-run on 1.4.2: install @sentry/hono plus @sentry/bun, call app.use(sentry(app, { dsn, tracesSampleRate: 1.0 })) on a fresh new Hono(), export { port, fetch: app.fetch }, and run bun app.ts. No instrument file and no --preload are needed, because the middleware calls Sentry.init itself. traceLifecycle defaults to stream on this line, which names the request span http.server GET /users/:id and the database span db SELECT users; under traceLifecycle: 'static' the same run gives a transaction GET /users/:id and a span db SELECT * FROM users. One Bun bug needs a workaround: Bun garbage-collects a node:diagnostics_channel channel that only Sentry's subscriber holds, so a driver whose first query happens later in the process silently loses its spans; pin the channels by keeping a strong reference right after the middleware is registered, globalThis.__sentryPinnedChannels ??= ['mysql2:query', 'mysql2:execute', 'mysql2:connect', 'mysql2:pool:connect'].map(name => dc.tracingChannel(name)), which is harmless on Node. Building the same entry with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.ts'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin and running bun dist/app.js adds spans for mysql@2; the bundled captures cover errors, route names, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch, both database drivers and logs, and do not drive the isolation route.

Works today:

  • Unhandled handler errors, with the route as the transaction name
  • Parameterized route names (GET /users/:id, source route)
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 3.20.0+ database spans with no build step, as long as the driver is loaded and queried early or the channels are pinned as above
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes, flushed on a roughly 5 second timer and needing no enableLogs option

Known limits:

  • Drivers that do not publish node:diagnostics_channel events themselves, such as mysql@2, produce spans only in the Bun.build bundle.
  • Do not pass external: ["@sentry/*"] to Bun.build for a Hono app. Externalizing @sentry/* while hono is inlined gives two copies of hono's GET_MATCH_RESULT symbol and every request 500s inside @sentry/hono's requestHandler. Either pass no external at all (the verified recipe), or externalize hono together with @sentry/*, which then behaves exactly like the unbundled run.

What to change:

  • guides/hono/index.mdx: add a "Run your app" step with a Bun tab showing bun app.ts and the Bun.build + sentryBunPlugin() variant, state that the --preload step is not needed here, and name the span names a reader will see under the stream default and under static.
  • guides/bun/index.mdx:90-94: rewrite the alert so it describes the plugin build as the supported way to instrument bundled code.
  • packages/hono/README.md:172-190: the Bun section ends in the Node recipe (serve from @hono/node-server); replace it with the export default app form the docs page shows.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F1, cell M-hono-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hono/index.mdx — add a "Run your app" step with a Bun tab (unbundled `bun app.ts` and the `Bun.build` + `sentryBunPlugin()` variant); and rewrite the alert at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx:90-94, which is contradicted by this run.

The Bun tab shows the `sentry(app, {...})` middleware but never shows how to start the app, and never states what works. The page must say that on Bun the app runs unbundled as `bun app.ts` (the `sentry()` middleware calls `Sentry.init` itself, so the `bun --preload ./instrument.js` step the Bun guide teaches at bun/index.mdx:100 is not needed here), and that in that shape errors, route names, request isolation, incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` continuation, outgoing `fetch` spans, `mysql2` db spans and logs all work. It must state the driver split rather than one rule: `mysql2` 3.20.0+ publishes its own `node:diagnostics_channel` events and needs no build step, while `mysql`@2 produces a span only after the app is built with `Bun.build({ target: 'bun', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] })` from `@sentry/bun/plugin` and run as `bun dist/app.js` — which directly contradicts the Bun guide's alert at bun/index.mdx:90-94 that "Sentry's auto-instrumentation does not work with bundled code". It must also carry the `external` rule the bundle run measured: externalizing `@sentry/*` while `hono` stays inlined 500s every request, and the working choices are no `external` at all or `hono` externalized together with `@sentry/*`. Neither `sentryBunPlugin`, `@sentry/bun/plugin` nor `Bun.build` appears anywhere in the docs today.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hono/index.mdx:12 ("supports Hono 4+ across multiple runtimes: Cloudflare Workers, Node.js, Bun, and Deno"); hono/index.mdx:84-86 (Bun tab: `npm install @sentry/bun`); hono/index.mdx:278-300 (Bun tab of the setup snippet: `import { sentry } from "@sentry/hono/bun"` at :280, `export default app;` at :299); docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx:90-94 ("Bundled Code Limitation" alert); bun/index.mdx:100 (`bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js`). The Hono guide still has no run step: its headings are Install, Configure, Verify, Next Steps.

What ran: Hono 4.13.7 (`hono@4`) with `@sentry/hono/bun` 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0 and re-run on 1.4.2, both unbundled (`bun app.ts`) and as a `Bun.build` bundle with `sentryBunPlugin`; drivers `mysql2` 3.24.3 and `mysql`@2. The `external` question was covered by probe X01 (verdict partial); no refutation run exists for this cell. Cell record M-hono-bun.

DOC-F2Elysia on Bun: documented partialP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Add a Bun database instrumentation section to the Elysia guide and fix four include statements

Description

The Bun wiring on the Elysia guide is correct; three statements around it are not. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx:4-5 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.elysia with fallbackGuide: javascript.bun, elysia/index.mdx:23-25 lists the prerequisites, elysia/index.mdx:56 shows bun add @sentry/elysia, and the alert at elysia/index.mdx:47-50 ("You do not need @elysiajs/opentelemetry") is confirmed by the run. The defects are in the includes: platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:1 opens this server guide with a BrowserTracing paragraph; javascript.elysia.mdx:5 claims the ops are request_handler.elysia and middleware.elysia where the observed ops are plain handler and middleware; and platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.elysia.mdx:25 says 'static' is "the default" where this line defaults to 'stream'. On top of that, nothing on the page or in its includes says how database and library instrumentation behaves on Bun.

Verified on Elysia 1.4 with @sentry/elysia 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0: Sentry.init either inline in the entry or in an instrument.ts imported first, Sentry.withElysia(new Elysia()) wrapping the fresh instance before any route is chained, then bun app.ts. No --preload, no @elysiajs/opentelemetry, and no build step for the base capabilities. For libraries that need the plugin build, build with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.ts'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin and run bun dist/app.js. Pass no external at all, and no packages: 'external' or packages: '*': the follow-up run recorded that any of those puts the instrumented packages back outside the bundle and the spans disappear again. sentryBunPlugin() is build-only; calling Bun.plugin(sentryBunPlugin()) from a --preload file crashes the app at import.

Works today:

  • Unhandled handler errors via the SDK's global onError hook
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route, on the success and error paths
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation, plus response trace headers
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans under a plain bun app.ts; database instrumentation as a whole is recorded partial, and this is the half that works
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • On Bun only libraries that publish node:diagnostics_channel events themselves are instrumented under a plain bun app.ts; every other instrumented library needs the plugin build above. The failure is silent: with debug: true the SDK logs "No available Node API to register diagnostics-channel injection hooks; skipping" and still reports "Integration installed: Mysql", and without debug there is no output at all.
  • Every event, transaction and span reports sdk.name: "sentry.javascript.bun", never sentry.javascript.elysia, under both init placements and both lifecycles.
  • Events captured inside a handler carry no transaction name, because the name is set after the handler runs.
  • The SDK's own hooks are visible in every trace as sentryOnRequest, sentryOnAfterHandle and sentryOnError spans, and the first pool use adds db mysql2.pool.connect and db mysql2.connect.

What to change:

  • guides/elysia/index.mdx: add a "Database and library instrumentation on Bun" section after Configure with the bundle recipe and the "no external" rule. Leave the alert at :47-50 as it is; the run confirms it.
  • platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:11: qualify "No additional runtime instrumentation needed" for the database and library case.
  • platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:1: drop the BrowserTracing paragraph from this server guide.
  • platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:5: the observed ops are handler and middleware, not request_handler.elysia and middleware.elysia.
  • platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.elysia.mdx:25: the traceLifecycle default is 'stream', not 'static'. DOC-R1 covers javascript.bun.mdx, javascript.deno.mdx and javascript.cloudflare.mdx, not this file.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F2, cell M-elysia-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx — new "Database and library instrumentation on Bun" section after Configure; plus four include fixes in platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx (:1, :5, :11) and platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.elysia.mdx:25.

The Bun-first wiring on this page is correct as published — errors, route names, isolation, incoming trace, outgoing fetch and logs all work following it, and the alert at :47-50 saying `@elysiajs/opentelemetry` is not needed is confirmed by the run, so it should stay. What is missing is the database/library limit observed on Bun: under a plain `bun app.ts` the SDK produces db spans only for drivers that publish `node:diagnostics_channel` events themselves (`mysql2` does, so it needs no build step); for `mysql`@2 and every other instrumented library (Express, Koa, Fastify, Hapi, AI SDKs) there are no spans at all unless the app is bundled with `Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.ts'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] })` from `@sentry/bun/plugin` and run as `bun dist/app.js`, with no `external` passed. The sentence that needs qualifying for that case is not the alert but platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:11, "**Bun**: ... No additional runtime instrumentation needed." Separately, the shipped SDK reports `sentry.javascript.bun` on every event, transaction and span, contradicting this page's own `sdk: sentry.javascript.elysia` frontmatter — the verified finding is that this is an SDK defect to fix in code, so the issue records it as a known limit rather than asking the docs to describe it, and the frontmatter should stay as-is.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx:4-5 (`sdk: sentry.javascript.elysia`, `fallbackGuide: javascript.bun`); elysia/index.mdx:23-25 (prerequisites: Elysia v1.4.0+, Bun or Node.js 18+); elysia/index.mdx:47-50 ("You do **not** need `@elysiajs/opentelemetry`. The `@sentry/elysia` SDK handles all instrumentation natively." — confirmed by the run); elysia/index.mdx:56 (`bun add @sentry/elysia`); platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:1, :5, :11; platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.elysia.mdx:25

What ran: Elysia 1.4 with @sentry/elysia 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0, unbundled and as a `Bun.build` bundle. Follow-up runs: verify4-M-elysia-bun-dbspans (partial, reproduced the driver split exactly) and verify4-M-elysia-bun-sdkname (confirmed-broken). Cell record M-elysia-bun.

DOC-F3Express on Bun: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add an Express on Bun page covering the Bun.build plugin step

Description

No guide page pairs Express with Bun. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/express/index.mdx:4-5 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.node with fallbackGuide: javascript.node and renders getting-started-node (express/index.mdx:12), whose only run shape is node --import ./instrument.mjs app.mjs (platform-includes/getting-started-node/javascript.mdx:88,95). The Bun guide teaches bun --preload ./instrument.js app.js (guides/bun/index.mdx:100) and names no framework. The pairing does exist elsewhere in the docs but never with instructions: 81 common integration pages list javascript.express and javascript.bun together in supported:, and common/configuration/integrations/mysql2.mdx:30 states "This integration only works in the Node.js and Bun runtimes".

Verified on Express 4.22.1 with @sentry/bun 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0 and re-run on 1.4.2: install @sentry/bun instead of @sentry/node, plus the mysql driver, put Sentry.init in instrument.ts imported as the first line of app.ts, create the database connection at module scope, keep plain app.listen(port), then build with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.ts'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', external: ['@sentry/*'], sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin and run bun dist/app.js. The external: ['@sentry/*'] entry is what lets the process-wide client from the unbundled instrument.ts and the injected channels inside the bundle agree on one client. It is specific to this app shape and must not be generalized to every Bun framework: the same flag is fatal for a Hono app (DOC-F1).

Works today:

  • Unhandled route errors, one event, no setupExpressErrorHandler call needed
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route, plus middleware and handler child spans
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql database spans
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Without the bundle, running bun app.ts or bun --preload ./instrument.ts app.ts gives URL-sourced transaction names, no middleware, handler or database spans, and no automatic error capture. The node:http server span and the http.client span for outgoing fetch still arrive, and so do logs, which is what makes the gap easy to miss.
  • The SDK emits no warning about the missing instrumentation unless debug: true is set.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/express.mdx: the @sentry/bun and mysql install, the instrument.ts + first-import placement, the build.ts recipe with its external entry and the warning not to reuse that entry for other frameworks, the bun dist/app.js run command, and the capability list above.
  • Keep Sentry.setupExpressErrorHandler(app) documented on that page for the unbundled quick start: it is the only thing that captures errors there, with mechanism auto.middleware.express rather than auto.http.express, and it is harmless in the bundled build.
  • State on that page that a missing instrumentation is silent without debug: true.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F3, cell M-express-bun

Description

The Bun claim already exists in the docs elsewhere — common/configuration/integrations/fastify.mdx says "This integration only works in the Node.js and Bun runtimes" — only the guide is missing.

Verified on Fastify 5.7.1 with @sentry/bun 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0 and re-run on 1.4.2: install @sentry/bun plus mysql2, put Sentry.init in instrument.mjs, import it as the first line of app.mjs or pass bun --preload ./instrument.mjs app.mjs, and run the app directly. No Bun.build step and no sentryBunPlugin() are needed for Fastify itself. For drivers that need code injection, build with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./app.mjs'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', external: ['@sentry/*'], sourcemap: 'external', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin and run bun dist/app.mjs; the follow-up run used external: ['@sentry/bun', 'mysql'] instead, which is equally safe because the plugin removes the packages it instruments from the external list again. sentryBunPlugin() is build-only: Bun.plugin(sentryBunPlugin()) in a --preload file crashes the app at import, so the bundle is the only route for injected drivers.

Works today:

  • Unhandled route-handler errors, attached to the request transaction
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route, plus two handler child spans per request (handler GET /users/:id and handler fastify - route-handler); the source is directly assertable under traceLifecycle: 'static'
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers; the client-span name differs by lifecycle
  • mysql2 3.20.0+ database spans with no build step, covering execute(), the promise wrapper and pool connections. Verified at mysql2 3.24.3 on Fastify 5.12.3 in the follow-up run; the original cell, which used only mysql@2, recorded database spans as partial. Unlike injected drivers, mysql2 does not need a module-scope connection: a connection created inside the request still gets spans, because the subscription happens at Sentry.init() time.
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Drivers Sentry instruments by code injection, mysql@2 among them, produce no span under a plain run; they need the bundle above, with the connection created at module scope.
  • The missing database span is silent: the query succeeds and returns rows, and the SDK says nothing unless debug: true is set, so the app looks fully instrumented while it has no database visibility.
  • Fastify still answers its own 500 body while the error is reported.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/fastify.mdx with the @sentry/bun and mysql2 install, instrument.mjs, run command and the capability list.
  • Rewrite guides/bun/index.mdx:90-94 into a "Database instrumentation on Bun" section that splits behaviour by driver rather than stating one rule, and says the failure is silent without debug: true.

DOC-F7Oak on Bun: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add an Oak on Bun page covering the Bun.serve routes map

Description

Oak appears nowhere in the docs, on any runtime. The Bun guide, docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx:1-212, names no framework and never mentions Bun.serve or a routes map, and docs/platforms/javascript/common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx:18 is the only description of the Bun.serve instrumentation but says nothing about how server spans are named. Bun.serve itself appears in the docs only on that integration page.

Verified on Oak 17.2.0 with @sentry/bun 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0. Oak is JSR-only, so installation needs an .npmrc with @jsr:registry=https://npm.jsr.io and npm install "@oak/oak@npm:@jsr/oak__oak@17.2.0", followed by npm install @sentry/bun mysql2; bun add @oak/oak fails outright and bun add could not resolve the SDK packages' transitive @sentry/* dependencies, so the npm route is required. Put Sentry.init in instrument.ts imported first, create the mysql2 connection at module scope, mount the app through Bun.serve, and run bun app.ts — no bundle and no sentryBunPlugin() were needed for any capability here. For route names, build the routes map from the Oak router at startup and keep fetch as the fallback: const handler = req => app.fetch(req); const routes = {}; for (const r of router) routes[r.path] = handler; Bun.serve({ port, routes, fetch: handler }). That map shape comes from the follow-up run; the cell app named spans with a router-level middleware instead.

Works today:

  • Unhandled handler errors, with mechanism auto.http.bun.serve; with an Oak error middleware in place the mechanism becomes oak, and a user-registered Oak error listener stops the re-throw, after which the app has to capture the error itself
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route and url.template once the routes map is passed, in both lifecycles and on the error path. The recorded verdict for route names is partial: see the naming limit below
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans with no build step, because mysql2 publishes its own node:diagnostics_channel events. This is the generalizable half of the Bun story: "no library spans without a bundle" is not a universal rule, but a driver without native channels still needs the sentryBunPlugin bundle
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes correlated to the request trace

Known limits:

  • Mounted with fetch only, server spans take the raw pathname with source url under traceLifecycle: 'static' (GET /users/7, high cardinality) and collapse to a bare GET under the stream default.
  • Even with the routes map, routes using regex constraints, optional params or (.*) are derived into the map and still fall back to source url (/api/opt from /api/opt/:a?, /api/star/a/b from /api/star/(.*)), while a path that Oak answers with a 404 can still be labelled GET /api/num/:id(\d+) with source route.
  • Mounted with app.listen() the SDK still emits method-only server spans, but errors are never captured, the database span is not emitted at all, the outgoing-fetch span detaches onto the process trace as its own root, logs carry a stale process trace id, and request isolation leaks across concurrent requests (all three concurrent events carried one tag and one trace id).
  • Neither naming approach works under app.listen(); bundling with @sentry/bun/plugin does not change route naming either, and neither does init placement or lifecycle.
  • Browser-side capture was not exercised.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/oak.mdx with the JSR install, the Bun.serve mount and routes map, the bun app.ts run command, and the capability list.
  • Document the second naming option beside the map: a router-level router.use middleware calling Sentry.updateSpanName and setting the sentry.segment.name.source attribute to route, which also names the error path. updateSpanName alone leaves the source as custom. The routes map is preferable because it also sets url.template and runs once at startup.
  • common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx: add that server span names come from the Bun.serve routes map.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F7, cell M-oak-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/oak.mdx (or guides/oak/index.mdx with fallbackGuide: javascript.bun, the Elysia pattern). Also: common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx must gain a section stating that server span names come from the Bun.serve routes map.

A page must say: Oak 17 runs on Bun mounted as `Bun.serve({ fetch: req => app.fetch(req) })` and started with `bun app.ts`. Install needs the JSR npm registry because Oak is JSR-only: an `.npmrc` with `@jsr:registry=https://npm.jsr.io` plus `npm install "@oak/oak@npm:@jsr/oak__oak@17.2.0"`, then `npm install @sentry/bun mysql2`. On Bun the server span name comes from the routes map passed to `Bun.serve`, so a fetch-only mount produces the raw pathname with source `url` under the static lifecycle and a bare `GET` under the stream default, bundled or not. Document the verified workaround: build a routes map from the Oak router at startup and keep `fetch` as the fallback, with the router-level `updateSpanName` middleware as the second option. `app.listen()` must be documented as a shape to avoid rather than one that does nothing: server spans do arrive, method-only, but errors are never captured, the database span is missing, the outgoing-`fetch` span detaches onto the process trace, logs carry a stale trace id and request isolation leaks between concurrent requests. Errors, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans, mysql2 database spans and logs all work in the `Bun.serve` shape. Client-side errors were not exercised and must not be claimed.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/index.mdx:1-212 (names no framework, never mentions Bun.serve or a routes map; guides/bun/ also holds configuration/integrations/contextlines.mdx); common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx:18 ("The `bunServerIntegration` instruments [`Bun.serve` API](...) to automatically create transactions and capture errors." — the only description of Bun.serve instrumentation, and it says nothing about span naming); grep for Oak and for the JSR npm registry over docs and platform-includes returns zero hits.

  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 database spans with no build plugin
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes
  • Route names GET /users/:id with source route, once a middleware registered before app.fsRoutes() renames the root span from ctx.route and sets the sentry.segment.name.source attribute to route. updateSpanName alone leaves the source as custom, and setAttribute('sentry.source', 'route') is the v10 key and is ignored on v11

Known limits:

  • Unhandled route and middleware errors are not captured automatically, because Fresh renders its own 500 before anything reaches Bun.serve. The request span is still sent with status internal_error, so the reader sees a failing transaction and no event. Report the error from one of three equivalent hooks: Sentry.captureException(ctx.error, { mechanism: { type: 'fresh._error', handled: false } }) in routes/_error.tsx; app.onError('*', ...) in main.ts, which needs no try/catch and re-throw; or a middleware registered before every other app.use. Do not rely on captureConsoleIntegration: it captures only while Fresh's built-in error page is in use and stops entirely once _error.tsx or onError exists.
  • The plugin build is not an alternative here. Fresh compiles the whole framework into server-entry.js and its App hard-codes the error hook to a no-op, so a Bun.build with sentryBunPlugin() does not change this, unlike the Bun framework pages that present that build as the fix.
  • Fresh's own OpenTelemetry spans never reach Sentry: every transaction arrived with spans: [] apart from the ones the SDK created.
  • Browser-side capture was not exercised, and /assets/* static-file serving, which still calls Deno.stat and Deno.open, was not exercised under Bun.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/fresh/index.mdx with the build and externals config, the entry.ts mount and bun entry.ts, the node_modules placement rule, the error hook, the naming middleware and the capability list. Which fallbackGuide it takes is a scoping decision this run does not settle, since Fresh was also probed on Deno and on Cloudflare.
  • common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx:18: qualify the automatic error capture claim.
  • common/enriching-events/transaction-name/index.mdx:90-103: document that sentry.segment.name.source is the v11 attribute for the name source and that the v10 sentry.source key is ignored.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F8, cell M-fresh-bun

Also change common/configuration/integrations/bunserver.mdx:18 to qualify automatic error capture, and common/enriching-events/transaction-name/index.mdx:90-103 to document sentry.segment.name.source.

Errors are NOT captured automatically, which contradicts the blanket claim on the BunServer page: Fresh's `App.handler()` catches every route and middleware error and renders its own 500, so nothing reaches `Bun.serve` and the reader is left with a transaction marked `internal_error` and no event; show `routes/_error.tsx` with `captureException` and an explicit mechanism, and name `app.onError('*')` and an early middleware as equivalent alternatives. Child spans are absent either way.
  • Initializing from @sentry/nestjs instead of @sentry/bun costs the server span entirely: unbundled, no server span of any kind is emitted and events lose their transaction name.
  • Without the SentryGlobalFilter provider, controller errors are swallowed and no event is sent.
  • There is no runtime hook that substitutes for the bundle. Bun.plugin(sentryBunPlugin()) at runtime breaks @nestjs/common's CommonJS named exports and the app does not boot (SyntaxError: Export named 'Injectable' not found), and @sentry/server-runtime-injection/register is a no-op on Bun because node:module.registerHooks is undefined there.

  • Browser-side capture was not exercised.

Install @sentry/bun alongside @sentry/nextjs and add its fetchIntegration() to integrations in sentry.server.config.ts. Database and cache spans need a second addition in the same file, a workaround for a Bun bug: Bun garbage-collects the node:diagnostics_channel objects that only Sentry's subscriber references, so every subscription made by Sentry.init() is gone a tick later. Keep a strong reference to them right after the init call:

import * as dc from 'node:diagnostics_channel'; globalThis.__sentryKeptChannels = ['mysql2:query', 'mysql2:execute', 'mysql2:connect', 'mysql2:pool:connect', 'ioredis:command', 'ioredis:connect'].map(name => dc.tracingChannel(name));

Works today:

  • Unhandled route-handler errors

  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans and propagation headers, with fetchIntegration() added
  • Database and cache spans for drivers that publish native diagnostics channels — mysql2 3.20.0 or later, ioredis 5.11.0 or later, node-redis 5.12.0 or later — once the channels are pinned as above, under both lifecycles
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Drivers below the native-channel cutoff stay unreachable on Next.js, because they need build-time injection and only ioredis is un-externalized for it.
  • There is no Sentry-specific Bun build step and none is possible: Next.js is built by webpack or turbopack, so the sentryBunPlugin() path other Bun framework pages rely on cannot be used here, and @sentry/nextjs/build/import-hook.mjs no-ops under Bun. The [Sentry] Runtime hook not registered line that reports this is only visible with debug: true.
  • contexts.runtime reports { name: 'node' } on every event even though process.versions.bun is set, so Bun events cannot be told apart from Node ones in Sentry.
  • Edge-runtime routes, middleware, server actions and browser-side capture were not exercised.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/nextjs.mdx with the install, the fetchIntegration() from @sentry/bun, the channel pin, the typescript 5.x requirement, the bun --bun build and start commands, and which instrumentation.ts branch runs under bun --bun.

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/nextjs.mdx (new), plus a "Project directory named `app`" troubleshooting note under docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/

A page must say Next.js 15.5.4 (app router) builds and serves under `bun --bun ./node_modules/.bin/next build` / `bun --bun ./node_modules/.bin/next start` and that `@sentry/nextjs` initializes there, then name the three things the Node path does not need. (1) Install `@sentry/bun` alongside `@sentry/nextjs` and add its `fetchIntegration()` to `integrations` in `sentry.server.config.ts`; without it outgoing `fetch` produces no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers, because the Node SDK's HTTP instrumentation relies on undici diagnostics channels Bun never publishes. (2) After `Sentry.init()`, keep a strong reference to the tracing channels (`mysql2:query`, `mysql2:execute`, `mysql2:connect`, `mysql2:pool:connect`, `ioredis:command`, `ioredis:connect`) on `globalThis` — Bun collects channels that only Sentry's subscriber references, so the subscriptions made at init are gone a tick later and no db span is recorded. This is a workaround for a Bun bug and is harmless on Node. (3) State which `instrumentation.ts` branch runs under `bun --bun`, since the documented `register()` at :122-134 covers only `nodejs` and `edge`. A separate troubleshooting note applies to Node and Bun equally: do not name the project root, or any ancestor of your `app/` directory, `app` — Next.js derives the route on its OpenTelemetry spans by splitting the route file's absolute path on the first `/app/` segment (`getPathnameFromAbsolutePath`), so an extra `app` segment prefixes the transaction name with a spurious `/app` and leaves `event.transaction` (`GET /api/users/[id]`) disagreeing with the transaction envelope (`GET /app/api/users/[id]`). The source stays `route`, and the whole thing reproduces byte for byte on plain Node, so it belongs in Next.js troubleshooting rather than on a Bun page. The repo's nextjs-16-bun e2e app additionally passes `bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false })`; that was not exercised in this run and isolation was recorded working without it, so the page should not require it.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nextjs/index.mdx:4 (`sdk: sentry.javascript.nextjs`); nextjs/index.mdx:51-53 (the Client/Server/Edge list; :53 is the Edge bullet); nextjs/index.mdx:122-134 (`instrumentation.ts` branches only on `NEXT_RUNTIME === "nodejs"` at :126 and `"edge"` at :129, with `onRequestError` at :134); nextjs/index.mdx:151 (still imports `withSentryConfig` from the old path); common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:1022 (the `@sentry/nextjs/config` move); platform-includes/logs/usage/javascript.nextjs.mdx:7 (implies Bun is a supported Next.js server runtime); docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/ never mentions Next.js.

Follow-up runs verify4-M-nextjs-bun-dbspans, -outgoingfetch and -routenames are all recorded works-after-all. The SDK-side fix for the channel bug is to retain the channel objects at the subscribe sites in `@sentry/server-utils`, which would remove the pin for every Bun user. Cell record M-nextjs-bun.

DOC-F11Nuxt on Bun: documented wrongP0proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Add a Bun section to the Nuxt Nitro deployment presets page

Description

docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7 says the presets "work out of the box" and nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:9-11 carves out only Cloudflare Pages, while the documented start command at guides/nuxt/index.mdx:72 is node --import ./.output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs .output/server/index.mjs. Nothing under guides/nuxt/ mentions Bun.

Verified on Nuxt 4.5.2 (Nitro 2.13.4) with @sentry/nuxt 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0: build with NITRO_PRESET=bun npx nuxt build and run with bun run .output/server/index.mjs. No inject mode and no start-up flag are needed: on develop, commit ec50b3e8f1 bundles sentry.server.config.ts into the Nitro build and imports it from a generated Nitro plugin, so Sentry.init runs at server startup by itself. Both autoInjectServerSentry modes are deprecated as of that commit and keep working until v12. The sentry.server.config.ts source file stays where it is, but it is no longer emitted to .output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs, which also makes the Node start command at nuxt/index.mdx:72 obsolete on both runtimes. What Bun does need is the server instrumentation: install @sentry/bun alongside @sentry/nuxt and pass integrations: [bunServerIntegration(), fetchIntegration()] from it to the existing Sentry.init in sentry.server.config.ts. The root cause is a runtime gap rather than a preset choice: Bun never publishes the http.server.request.start diagnostics-channel event the Node instrumentation opens the request span from (measured: 0 events on Bun against 1 on Node), so no preset avoids it. bunServerIntegration() is the right integration for the bun preset, which serves through Bun.serve; the default node-server preset also runs under Bun, yields zero spans, and needs bunHttpServerIntegration() instead. The capability list below rests on the three recorded follow-up runs, which all prescribe bunServerIntegration() alone; the pairing with fetchIntegration() was exercised in a further run whose result was never recorded, and that run also observed that with fetchIntegration() alone the http.client span is a root span rather than a child of the request.

Works today, with bunServerIntegration() added:

  • Unhandled server-route errors via the Nitro error hook, with or without the extra integrations
  • http.server spans named GET /users/:id with source route, http.route, and the url.path.parameter.id and params.id attributes
  • Per-request isolation and a fresh trace per request
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • mysql2 database spans, provided nitro: { externals: { external: ['mysql2'] } } is set in nuxt.config.ts
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes
  • Events keep sdk: sentry.javascript.nuxt and the Nuxt event processors throughout

Known limits:

  • Without bunServerIntegration() there is no request span at all: every event in the process shares one trace id, a stale transaction name leaks from an unrelated earlier request onto every later event, names are not route-based, incoming trace headers are ignored, and the mysql2 span is dropped as well, because its subscriber is bound with requiresParentSpan: true and has no request span to attach to.
  • The nitro.externals.external entry and the database span are two separate problems. Without the entry the query itself fails with genFunc$1 is not a function, with or without Sentry; with the entry but without bunServerIntegration() the query succeeds and no span is recorded.
  • Drivers that rely on Sentry's build-time channel injection, mysql v2 among them, must stay inside the Nitro bundle. Bun installs no runtime require hook, so an externalized driver of that kind is never instrumented.
  • Unparameterized routes stay method-only (GET) under the stream default and take the URL path (GET /api/ping) under traceLifecycle: 'static'; the source is url in both.
  • Manually captured events still carry no transaction field, even after the request span exists.

What to change:

  • guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx: add a Bun section next to the Cloudflare Pages one covering NITRO_PRESET=bun npx nuxt build, bun run .output/server/index.mjs, the two @sentry/bun integrations (with bunHttpServerIntegration() as the node-server variant) and the Nitro externals rule.
  • guides/nuxt/index.mdx:72: the node --import ./.output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs command no longer matches the SDK, which bundles the server config into the Nitro build; replace it and add bun run .output/server/index.mjs for Bun.
  • guides/nuxt/install/limited-server-tracing.mdx:32 documents autoInjectServerSentry: 'top-level-import', which is now deprecated, and the warning at :16-23 and :19 claims that mode "will not capture data from database calls, queues, ORMs, third-party libraries" — a Node control run with exactly that setting produced db SELECT users, db mysql2.pool.connect and http.client spans, so the restriction as written is stronger than the SDK's behaviour.
  • Document the alternative and its cost: replacing the whole init with @sentry/bun in sentry.server.config.ts produces the same spans, but events then report sdk: sentry.javascript.bun and the Nuxt event processors are dropped.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F11, cell M-nuxt-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx — new "Bun" section alongside the Cloudflare Pages one (and a Bun start command replacing docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/index.mdx:72)

The presets page's "work out of the box" claim, with only Cloudflare excepted, is contradicted by the `bun` Nitro preset run under `bun run .output/server/index.mjs`: `@sentry/nuxt` uses the Node HTTP instrumentation, which never sees `Bun.serve`, so with the documented setup alone there is no request span — every event shares one trace and one scope across concurrent requests, later events carry the transaction name of an unrelated earlier request, transactions are not route-named, incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` is not continued, and the mysql2 span is suppressed for want of a parent. The page must add a Bun section stating that you install `@sentry/bun` alongside `@sentry/nuxt` and add `bunServerIntegration()` from it to `integrations` in `sentry.server.config.ts` (`bunHttpServerIntegration()` on the `node-server` preset), which restores `http.server` segment spans named `GET /users/:id` with `sentry.segment.name.source=route`, per-request trace ids, per-request isolation and the database span, while keeping the `sentry.javascript.nuxt` SDK identity. For the database it must additionally say to stop Nitro from bundling `mysql2` in `nuxt.config.ts` — otherwise every query 500s with `genFunc$1 is not a function`, with or without Sentry — while drivers that need Sentry's build-time injection must stay bundled. The start command at nuxt/index.mdx:72 needs replacing on both runtimes: develop bundles `sentry.server.config.ts` into the Nitro build (commit ec50b3e8f1), so there is no `.output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs` to `--import` any more, and on Bun the command is `bun run .output/server/index.mjs`. On the SDK side, the reason this needs a cross-package import at all is that `patchEventHandler` clones the isolation scope without resetting its propagation context; making it detect `process.versions.bun` would remove the need for `@sentry/bun` in a Nuxt app, and `bunServerIntegration()` is otherwise an undocumented cross-package import for Nuxt users.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7 ("provides support for different Nitro deployment presets, most of which **work out of the box**"); nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:9-11 (Cloudflare Pages is the only preset carved out; the file is 11 lines); docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/index.mdx:72 (`node --import ./.output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs .output/server/index.mjs` as the documented start command); guides/nuxt/install/limited-server-tracing.mdx:19 and :32. `grep -i bun` over guides/nuxt/ and `grep -i nuxt` over guides/bun/ both return 0.

What ran: Nuxt 4.5.2 (Nitro 2.13.4, h3 v1) with @sentry/nuxt 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0, nitro `bun` preset served through Bun.serve. Put a root sentry.server.config.mjs guarded with Sentry.isInitialized(), run astro build, then bun --preload ./sentry.server.config.mjs dist/server/entry.mjs. The preload is required, because the integration injects the init only into .astro page chunks, so an app whose first request hits an API route would otherwise run uninstrumented. Install @sentry/bun and add its fetchIntegration() to the server init, guarded on typeof globalThis.Bun !== 'undefined' so Node deployments of the same build do not double-count. Database spans need one more workaround for a Bun bug: Bun's node:diagnostics_channel does not keep a channel alive once it has subscribers, so the SDK reads hasSubscribers = true while the injected wrapper reads false. Pin the channels on globalThis next to Sentry.init() in sentry.server.config.mjs — globalThis.__sentryPinnedChannels ??= ['mysql2:query', 'mysql2:execute', 'mysql2:connect', 'mysql2:pool:connect', 'orchestrion:mysql:query', 'orchestrion:mysql2:query', 'orchestrion:mysql2:execute'].map(name => dc.tracingChannel(name)) — which is harmless on Node and can be applied unconditionally. Loading the driver early is not a substitute here: it fixes mysql2 and not mysql@2.

Works today:

  • Unhandled endpoint errors via the Astro request middleware, mechanism auto.middleware.astro
  • Route names GET /users/[id] with source route and http.route
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans and propagation headers, with fetchIntegration() added
  • mysql2 and mysql@2 database spans on both lifecycles and both mysql2 code paths, with the channel pin above
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • The build needs a @sentry/bundler-plugins matching this SDK line; with an older one npx astro build dies with "The requested module @sentry/core does not provide an export named ServerRuntimeClient".
  • contexts.runtime reports node on Bun, so Bun events cannot be told apart from Node ones in Sentry.
  • ContextLines prints Failed to read file: native once per captured event on Bun.
  • Errors thrown while an .astro page renders were not exercised; the error probe is an endpoint. Browser-side capture was not exercised either, because the run used enabled: { client: false, server: true }.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F13, cell M-astro-bun

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/astro/index.mdx:21-29 — amend the runtime alert to name Bun, and add a "Running on Bun" section covering the fetch integration and the diagnostics-channel pin

The runtime alert tells a reader that a non-Node runtime is unsupported, but Astro 7.3.1's `@astrojs/node` standalone build (`dist/server/entry.mjs`) runs on Bun with `@sentry/astro` and delivers working endpoint errors, route names, request isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs. The alert must be amended to name Bun explicitly as working on the Node adapter output, and the page must state the two caveats observed. Outgoing `fetch` is not instrumented under Bun, because `@sentry/node`'s HTTP integration listens on undici's `undici:request:*` diagnostics channels and Bun's native fetch does not publish them — install `@sentry/bun` and add its `fetchIntegration()` to `sentry.server.config.mjs`, guarded on `typeof globalThis.Bun !== 'undefined'` so Node deployments of the same build do not double-span. Database spans are dropped because Bun holds diagnostics channels weakly and a channel with subscribers is not kept alive, so every subscription made at `Sentry.init()` is lost on the next tick; the workaround for that Bun bug is to keep the channels reachable by storing them on `globalThis` after `Sentry.init()` (`mysql2:query`, `mysql2:execute`, `mysql2:connect`, `mysql2:pool:connect`, `orchestrion:mysql:query`, `orchestrion:mysql2:query`, `orchestrion:mysql2:execute`), which is harmless on Node and therefore safe to apply unconditionally. The SDK-side fix is for the subscribe sites to retain the channels they subscribe to, which would remove the pin for every Bun user.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/astro/index.mdx:21-29 (the `<Alert level="warning" title="What runtime do you use?">` block; quoted text at :23-27 — "This SDK currently only works on Node runtimes, such as Node adapter or Vercel with Lambda functions... Other non-Node runtimes, like Vercel's Edge runtime, are currently **not supported**."); astro/index.mdx:4 (`sdk: sentry.javascript.astro`). `grep -i bun` over guides/astro/ and `grep -i astro` over guides/bun/ both return nothing.

What ran: Astro 7.3.1 with @astrojs/node ^11.1.5 standalone adapter and @sentry/astro 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0 and re-run on 1.4.2 (darwin arm64), running the Node-adapter build output `dist/server/entry.mjs`. The database result comes from verify4-M-astro-bun-dbspans (works-after-all), which also proved the root cause with a minimal repro (`afterGC A: false` on Bun against `true` on Node). On the re-run the Node control also produced `spans: []` for `GET /db`, so on that machine the database gap was not Bun-specific. Cell record M-astro-bun.

  • The debug line "No diagnostics-channel injection detected. Channel-based integrations will not record spans" still fires after the fix and is misleading.
  • contexts.runtime.name is node on Bun.
  • Action errors and browser-side capture were not exercised; the error probe is a loader.

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/react-router.mdx (new)

A page must say React Router 8 framework mode with a custom Express/Node server runs on Bun but that the stock `node-custom-server` template's `server.js` and `entry.server.tsx` have to be rewritten for Bun, and it must be exact about what each piece buys. With the documented setup alone the server SDK sends only errors and logs on Bun: no route names, no per-request isolation, no incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` continuation, no outgoing `fetch` spans and no database spans (child spans are additionally suppressed by `requiresParentSpan` once no request span exists). Two additions fix it, and both belong on the page. `export const instrumentations = [Sentry.createSentryServerInstrumentation()];` in the server entry — already documented at manual-setup.mdx:273 as optional for Node — is on its own enough to restore route-named `http.server` spans, because Bun has no diagnostics-channel injection for `node:http` and this is the only remaining source of server spans. `bunHttpServerIntegration()` from `@sentry/bun`, installed at the same version and deduped to one `@sentry/core`, restores isolation, incoming-trace continuation, outgoing-fetch spans and database spans; pass it together with `fetchIntegration()` behind a `process.versions.bun` gate, because the same build on Node otherwise emits two client spans per fetch. The Node-only start commands at manual-setup.mdx:400-401 need a documented Bun equivalent, and the `getMetaTagTransformer` escape hatch at :284-330 needs a `renderToReadableStream` variant. The SDK-side fix would be for `@sentry/node`'s HTTP server instrumentation to fall back to patching `http.Server.prototype.emit` when `process.versions.bun` is set, which would remove the extra package entirely.

Two of them disagree on library spans: the incoming-trace run reports that `bunHttpServerIntegration()` does not bring back express or database spans without a Bun bundle, while the database run records mysql2 spans with no bundle at all. verify4-M-reactrouter-bun-dbspans-v2 has a directory but no recorded result and no usable output.   • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage is recorded broken: the headers were ignored and the event kept the process trace id.
  • Outgoing fetch spans are recorded broken with the wiring above.

  • Follow-up runs for those four capabilities were carried out with bunHttpServerIntegration() from @sentry/bun added to Sentry.init, and their results were never recorded, so none of the four is stated here as fixed. Their raw output is suggestive, showing an http.client span with populated propagation headers under GET /api/fetch and a root span named GET /api/user/$id from the Vite plugin's inlined route patterns, but it comes from a different app shape and needs one recorded run before a page can promise it.

What to change:

  • tanstackstart-react/manual-setup/index.mdx: add a Bun section giving bun --preload ./instrument.server.mjs .output/server/index.mjs next to the command at :358, the channel pin with its stream-lifecycle note, and the limits above.
  • State on that page that the "No available Node API to register diagnostics-channel injection hooks" warning Bun prints is a false alarm once the Vite plugin has done the build-time injection, so it should not be read as an error.

Verified on Remix v2 (@remix-run/* ^2.17.5, the @remix-run/dev Vite plugin with the @remix-run/express adapter, express 4, vite 5) and @sentry/remix 10.67.0 (develop, 6f2da7483d) on Bun 1.4.0 and re-run on 1.4.2, driver mysql2 3.24.3: instrument.mjs imported as the first line of server.mjs, export const handleError = Sentry.sentryHandleError in app/entry.server.tsx, sentryRemixVitePlugin() from the @sentry/remix/vite subpath in vite.config.ts (it moved there in v11; see the migration guide at :1287-1294), the mysql2 pool at module scope, then bundle the server entry with Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./server.mjs'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin(), keepExternal] }) from @sentry/bun/plugin and run bun dist/server.js. Express is kept external by a small plugin placed after sentryBunPlugin() — { name: 'keep-external', setup(b) { b.config.external = [...(b.config.external ?? []), 'express']; } } — not by an external array entry, because the Sentry plugin removes the packages it instruments from that list; the route-names run reached a working state without externalizing express at all, so the two recorded runs differ here. Because the bundle inlines @remix-run/server-runtime, Sentry's createRequestHandler patch never runs, so hold the adapter handler in a variable and wrap it yourself: app.all('*', (req, res, next) => Sentry.withIsolationScope(scope => { scope.setSDKProcessingMetadata({ normalizedRequest: { url, method: req.method, headers: req.headers } }); return Sentry.continueTrace({ sentryTrace, baggage }, () => remixHandler(req, res, next)); })). The setSDKProcessingMetadata call is what puts request data on events.

Works today:

  • Unhandled loader errors via the handleError hook
  • Route names GET users/:id with source route — no leading slash — plus function LOADER routes/users.$id children, identical on the Node control
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation, with the wrapper above
  • mysql2 database spans
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes

Known limits:

  • Plain bun server.mjs sends errors and logs only, with no transactions or spans at all, and its events carry no transaction field, so errors group without a route. Isolation and incoming-trace continuation do work in that mode, and so do trace headers on outgoing fetch; adding fetchIntegration() from @sentry/bun also gives it an http.client span under the stream default, while under static the parentless client span is dropped. The repo's own e2e app create-remix-app-express/server.mjs uses this wiring and would produce zero spans on Bun.
  • Outgoing fetch spans in the bundled shape did not reproduce on the re-run: the recorder saw sentryTrace: null, baggage: null on every attempt, in both modes.
  • The bundle is target: 'bun' and cannot be run under Node.
  • The bundle is emitted without sourcemaps unless you enable them, so events lose context lines. sourcemap: 'external' is the expected fix and was not exercised.
  • Re-externalizing @remix-run/* instead of express restores isolation natively but removes every span, database spans included; bundling and transforming express breaks AsyncLocalStorage propagation across next() on Bun.
  • Browser-side capture was not exercised.

A page must say Remix v2 (`@remix-run/dev` vite plugin + `@remix-run/express` adapter) builds and runs on Bun and `@sentry/remix` initializes there (`Sentry.isInitialized() === true`), then pick one of the two run shapes and disclose what the other loses. Plain `bun server.mjs` must be documented as giving errors and logs only — no transactions and no spans at all, not even a request span — with events that carry no transaction name, so errors group without a route; isolation and incoming-trace continuation do work there. The working shape is to bundle the server entry with `Bun.build({ entrypoints: ['./server.mjs'], outdir: './dist', target: 'bun', plugins: [sentryBunPlugin()] })` from `@sentry/bun/plugin` and run `bun dist/server.js`; that yields route-named `http.server` transactions (`GET users/:id`, source `route`), `LOADER routes/users.$id` children and database spans. The root cause is that the CJS `loadModule('@remix-run/server-runtime', module)` monkeypatch and `withoutInstrumentedExternals` pull in opposite directions, so on Bun you can have spans or isolation but not both without the manual wrapper; an SDK-side fix there would remove the wrapper. The same is true of the `bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false })` alternative to the wrapper: unrecorded, and the file that describes it also notes that `spans: true` names the transaction `GET *` and that plain `bun server-bunhttp.mjs` puts error events on a different trace id than the transaction.

Database spans need a workaround for a Bun bug: Bun garbage-collects the SDK's diagnostics_channel subscriptions, so pin the channels on globalThis after Sentry.init() in server/plugins/sentry.ts — globalThis.__sentryPinnedChannels = ['mysql2:query', 'mysql2:execute', 'mysql2:connect', 'mysql2:pool:connect', 'orchestrion:mysql:query'].map(n => dc.tracingChannel(n)) — or use an equivalent bun --preload pin.mjs file if the Nitro plugin cannot be touched.

Works today:

  • Per-request isolation, one scope and one trace per request
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation
  • Outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers
  • mysql2 3.20.0+ and mysql v2 database spans alike, once the tracing channels are pinned. No build step and no driver import order is required; every channel-based integration on Bun is exposed to the same bug, not only databases
  • Sentry.logger log envelopes
  • Errors from server functions wrapped in withServerActionInstrumentation, and errors thrown in a file API route once the h3 middleware below is added. The recorded verdict for errors is partial: page-render errors stay dark either way

Known limits:

  • Errors thrown while a page component renders on the server are not reported, on Bun and on Node alike. File API route errors are reported only if src/middleware.ts wraps next() in a try/catch that calls Sentry.captureException(error, { mechanism: { handled: false, type: 'solidstart.middleware' } }) and re-throws — createMiddleware accepts a raw h3 middleware array on SolidStart 2, so this composes with sentryBeforeResponseMiddleware(). The guide's own Verify step at solidstart/index.mdx:444-453, a sentry-test.tsx route throwing from GET, is exactly the case that produces no event without it.
  • Server transactions are named from the raw URL (GET /users/456, source url) rather than the route pattern, on Bun and on Node alike, and under the stream default the segment collapses further to a bare GET. App code can fix the name: call getRootSpan(getActiveSpan()).updateName(...) and set http.route and sentry.segment.name.source, verified on both runtimes and both lifecycles.
  • bunHttpServerIntegration({ spans: false }) also isolates requests and continues traces but drops the node:http server span; the default is the parity choice. The NITRO_PRESET=bun plus bunServerIntegration() path was tried and rejected.
  • @sentry/bun adds roughly 2.4 MB to the server bundle.
  • contexts.runtime.name is node under Bun.

What to change:

  • New page docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/solidstart.mdx with the bun .output/server/index.mjs start command, the full SolidStart 2 Vite config, the two @sentry/bun integrations with the runtime guard, the channel pin and the capability list.

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/bun/frameworks/solidstart.mdx (new); plus route-naming, unreported-server-error and SolidStart 2 setup notes on docs/platforms/javascript/guides/solidstart/index.mdx

A page must say the SolidStart 2 nitro node output runs on Bun as `bun .output/server/index.mjs` and give the Bun equivalent of the `node --import ./.output/server/instrument.server.mjs` start command documented at index.mdx:297-298. It must state that on Bun, with the documented setup alone, requests are not isolated (every request shares one trace and one scope, so tags and user leak between concurrent requests), incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` is not continued, and outgoing `fetch()` produces no `http.client` span and sends no trace headers — because Bun does not emit the `http.server.request.start` diagnostics channel and its native fetch does not publish undici's channels. The fix: install `@sentry/bun` alongside `@sentry/solidstart` and add `bunHttpServerIntegration()` and `fetchIntegration()` to the `Sentry.init()` in `server/plugins/sentry.ts`, guarding the fetch one on `typeof Bun !== 'undefined'` so a Node deployment of the same build does not get two spans.
