Two further observed facts hold on Node as well and belong on the SolidStart guide itself: server transactions are named from the raw request URL (`GET /users/456`, `source: "url"`, collapsing to a bare `GET` under the stream default) rather than the route pattern, because SolidStart mounts its whole app as a single Nitro catch-all so the SDK's Nitro route parameterization never fires; and errors thrown in a file API route (`src/routes/api/*.ts`) or during page render are turned into a 500 by SolidStart's internal h3 app before nitro's error hook, so no event is sent — `withServerActionInstrumentation` or an h3 middleware registered through `createMiddleware` captures the API-route half, and page-render errors need an error boundary. The SDK-side fix for the channel bug is for `bindTracingChannelToSpan` to retain the channel it subscribes to, which was verified to work and would remove the pin.

Document the verified Hono on Deno setup and its outgoing fetch limit

Description

The page carries no runtime caveats around :360, and docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 never mentions node_modules, nodeModulesDir, the @sentry/deno/import hook or outgoing fetch.

Verified on Hono 4.13.7 with @sentry/hono/deno 10.67.0 on Deno 2.9.0: install @sentry/hono and @sentry/deno into a real on-disk node_modules with npm install, set {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"} in deno.json, put import "@sentry/deno/import"; as the first line of the entry file when you want database and library spans, then wire the middleware exactly as the current snippet shows and run deno run --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys main.ts.

Works today:

  • Unhandled route errors, with transaction: "GET /error" and mechanism auto.http.hono.context_error. Hono turns the throw into its own 500 response and the middleware's responseHandler reads context.error, so the event still arrives and the server span is marked internal_error.

  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests.
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql (requires the import hook line).
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented on Deno: no http.client span and no sentry-trace/baggage headers, so the downstream service starts a new trace. The default integrations of @sentry/deno carry denoHttpIntegration() for node:http/node:https and nothing that traces fetch; the breadcrumb integration patches fetch, so the call is still breadcrumbed while no span is started and no header is injected.
  • node:http/node:https clients are traced normally.
  • Without the import hook line, database and library spans are silently missing.

What to change:

  • guides/hono/index.mdx:88-89: show the deno.json and the npm-based install, not deno add alone.

Two things must be added. (1) Install: `deno add npm:@sentry/deno` at :89 is not sufficient for the observed working setup, which needed the packages installed with `npm install` into the project so a real on-disk `node_modules` exists, plus `{"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}` in `deno.json`, because the Deno SDK's import hook resolves through a local `node_modules`. The Deno tab must show the `deno.json` and the npm-based install, not just `deno add`. (2) Outgoing HTTP: a Deno tab caveat under tracing must state that `@sentry/hono/deno` does not instrument the global `fetch()` — an outgoing `fetch()` produces no `http.client` span and receives no `sentry-trace` or `baggage` header, so the downstream service starts a new trace; only `node:http`/`node:https` clients are traced automatically, through `denoHttpIntegration()`. This was re-verified as confirmed-broken in both `static` and `stream` trace lifecycles and with the Deno import hook both on and off. The cause sits below Hono: `@sentry/deno`'s default integration set contains no fetch tracing integration at all, while its breadcrumb integration already installs a fetch instrumentation handler, so the hook fires with no subscriber to start a span or inject headers. SDK-side, the missing piece exists as `fetchIntegration` in @sentry/cloudflare and could be ported to @sentry/deno; the same gap was closed on Deno with shipped exports in the Fastify (`winterCGFetchIntegration()` from @sentry/vercel-edge), Express and SvelteKit (`fetchIntegration()` from @sentry/cloudflare) cells, but neither was run against Hono, so this page states the gap as a limit and names no remedy.

Description

Verified on Elysia 1.4.30 and Deno 2.9.0: install the packages with npm into a real node_modules, set {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"} in deno.json, and pick one of two wirings. (1) Call Sentry.init() from @sentry/deno before Deno.serve and import only withElysia (and fetchIntegration) from @sentry/elysia. (2) Keep Sentry.init() from @sentry/elysia and pass integrations: [denoServeIntegration()], imported from @sentry/deno. Both need @sentry/deno installed alongside @sentry/elysia; both were exercised in the static and stream trace lifecycles. Mount with Deno.serve({ port }, withElysia(new Elysia()).get(...).fetch). Works today:

  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation, with either wiring.

  • Database spans for mysql, and logs.

Known limits:

  • @sentry/elysia's own init() on its own delegates to @sentry/bun and does not instrument Deno.serve: there is no http.server span, every Elysia lifecycle phase becomes its own root transaction on one process-wide trace, incoming trace headers are ignored and the isolation scope carries the previous request's route into the next event. An outgoing fetch still produces an http.client span under the default stream lifecycle, but as a parentless segment; under static it is dropped for having no active span.
  • The wiring decides the reported SDK: with Sentry.init from @sentry/deno events carry sdk.name: sentry.javascript.deno; with the denoServeIntegration() variant they keep sentry.javascript.bun and contexts.runtime.name: bun, and sessions are emitted.
  • mysql2 cannot be imported while the Deno import hook is active: Deno compiles require('*.json') as JavaScript (denoland/deno#36240) and the import throws SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':'. Use mysql@2.

What to change:

  • guides/elysia/index.mdx:25 and the snippets at :52-70, :78-120: add Deno tabs with both wirings described above.
  • platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:6,9-12: add a Deno bullet and qualify the route-name claim.

A page must state that Elysia 1.4 runs unchanged on Deno 2.9, mounted with Deno.serve and app.fetch, with nodeModulesDir manual and deps installed with npm, and that the wiring the guide shows today is wrong for this runtime. With Sentry.init from @sentry/elysia alone, route names, isolation and incoming trace continuation are all broken on Deno, because that init delegates to @sentry/bun, which does not instrument Deno.serve; every Elysia lifecycle phase becomes its own root transaction. Two wirings fix it, both built from shipped exports and both verified in the static and stream lifecycles: init from @sentry/deno and import only `withElysia` from @sentry/elysia (this also gets Deno's default integrations and tags events `sentry.javascript.deno`), or keep the @sentry/elysia init and add `denoServeIntegration()` from @sentry/deno (events then still self-report `sentry.javascript.bun` and `contexts.runtime.name: bun`). Neither needs a deep import into `build/esm`. Separately, the Deno SDK does not instrument global fetch; add `fetchIntegration()` — a real @sentry/elysia export, re-exported from @sentry/bun, not a hand-written shim — for http.client spans and trace headers. With that wiring, errors, database spans and logs work. The parameterized-route-names claim and the two-runtime list must both be corrected, and so must the fetchIntegration page, which still says the integration only works inside Cloudflare Workers.

Where the docs stand: guides/elysia/index.mdx:25 (lists only Bun and Node 18+ with the node adapter); :52-70 install snippet has only Bun and Node tabs; :78-120 configure snippet shows Sentry.init from @sentry/elysia, which delegates to @sentry/bun and does not instrument Deno.serve; platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:9-12 enumerates exactly two runtimes and :6 claims parameterized route names; grep for deno over guides/elysia returns zero; common/configuration/integrations/fetchIntegration.mdx:1-12 is scoped to javascript.cloudflare and says at :10 that it only works inside the Cloudflare Workers runtime.

DOC-F20Express on Deno: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add an Express on Deno page covering the plain setup and the import-hook setup

Description

Nothing covers this combination: grep -rni express docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/ and grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/express/ both return zero hits. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/express/index.mdx:4-5 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.node with fallbackGuide: javascript.node, and :12 is a bare <PlatformContent includePath="getting-started-node" />, so the whole guide assumes @sentry/node on Node. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 names no framework.

Verified on Express 5.2.1 and Deno 2.9.0 in two shapes. Both start the same way: npm install express@5 mysql@2 plus @sentry/deno into a real on-disk node_modules, and a deno.json with {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}; the app runs with deno run --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys app.ts. Shape A is the plain one: an instrument.ts that calls Sentry.init({ dsn, tracesSampleRate: 1.0 }) from @sentry/deno and is the first import of the app file. Shape B adds library instrumentation: the same file starts with import "@sentry/deno/import";, then imports a six-line JSON load hook, then inits. The load hook is Module.registerHooks({ load(url, context, nextLoad) { const result = nextLoad(url, context); if (url.endsWith(".json") && result.format !== "json") result.format = "json"; return result; } }) from node:module, registered after the Sentry hook; without it Express never imports. No integrations entry is needed in either shape, because expressIntegration() is already in the Deno SDK's default set.

Works today:

  • Both shapes: incoming requests produce http.server transactions, and Sentry.logger logs arrive.
  • Shape B: parameterized names GET /users/:id with source route, plus a handler /users/:id child span.
  • Shape B: unhandled route errors as events with mechanism auto.http.express and transaction: "GET /error"; Express still answers 500.
  • Shape B: database spans for a module-scope mysql@2 connection, as a child of the handler span.
  • Shape B: per-request isolation across concurrent requests with distinct trace ids, and incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation. Both were measured on this shape only.
  • Shape A error capture, with setupExpressErrorHandler(app) imported from @sentry/server-utils: route errors arrive with mechanism auto.middleware.express.
  • Outgoing fetch(), with fetchIntegration() imported from @sentry/cloudflare and passed to Sentry.init: http.client spans with origin auto.http.fetch parented under the handler span, and sentry-trace/baggage on the wire, in both trace lifecycles. It means installing @sentry/cloudflare in a Deno app.

Known limits:

  • Shape A names transactions GET /users/42 with source url and produces no handler spans and no database spans.
  • Shape A captures nothing for a thrown route error unless setupExpressErrorHandler is added: Express's default error handler swallows the throw and the SDK prints no warning, so the 500 is invisible in Sentry.
  • setupExpressErrorHandler has to come from @sentry/server-utils; @sentry/deno does not re-export it.
  • import "@sentry/deno/import" needs the companion JSON load hook because of denoland/deno#36240: with any module load hook registered, require('*.json') compiles as JavaScript, and Express dies at import time on require('./codes.json') inside statuses.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented by default in either shape: the Deno SDK's default integrations trace only node:http/node:https, so without the integration above a handler's fetch() gets no span and sends no trace headers.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/express.mdx with the install, deno.json, both init shapes including the JSON load hook, the run command and the capability split above.
  • Add a shared "Outgoing fetch requests" note to guides/deno/index.mdx.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F20, cell M-express-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/express.mdx (new), plus a new "The Deno import hook" section in guides/deno/index.mdx (currently absent from :1-230) that the framework pages link to

An Express-on-Deno page must exist, and it has to describe two setups, because the difference between them decides whether a 500 is visible in Sentry. Setup A is the one a reader would write from the current Deno guide: `Sentry.init` from `@sentry/deno` in an instrumentation file imported first. It gives `http.server` transactions, isolation and logs, but names them `GET /users/42` with source `url`, produces no handler and no database spans, and — the finding that matters — captures nothing at all when a route handler throws, because Express's default error handler swallows it and no warning is printed. Adding `setupExpressErrorHandler(app)` from `@sentry/server-utils` restores capture on that setup with mechanism `auto.middleware.express`; SDK-side, `@sentry/deno` not re-exporting it is what keeps this off the supported path. Setup B is `import "@sentry/deno/import"` plus a six-line `Module.registerHooks({ load })` that returns `format: "json"` for `.json` URLs, registered after it. The hook is not unusable on Deno 2.9, it just needs that companion: without it denoland/deno#36240 makes `require('*.json')` compile as JavaScript and Express dies at import in `statuses`. With both lines in place the page can promise `GET /users/:id` with source `route`, `handler` spans, `auto.http.express` error events and `db SELECT ...` spans, and the isolation and incoming-trace behaviour was measured on exactly this shape. Neither setup needs an `integrations: []` entry for Express itself, since `expressIntegration()` ships in the Deno default set. The remaining gap is outgoing HTTP: `@sentry/deno`'s defaults trace only `node:http`/`node:https`, so a native `fetch()` gets no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers. That is fixable today with a shipped export — `fetchIntegration()` from `@sentry/cloudflare` produced the span and both headers on Deno with no custom code in both lifecycles — at the cost of installing the Cloudflare SDK next to the Deno one; SDK-side the same file could be shipped from `@sentry/deno` instead, which would remove that cost.

Where the docs stand: `grep -rni express docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/` returns nothing and `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/express/` returns 0 hits; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/express/index.mdx:4-5 declares `sdk: sentry.javascript.node` / `fallbackGuide: javascript.node` and :12 is a bare `<PlatformContent includePath="getting-started-node" />`, i.e. the whole guide is @sentry/node on Node; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 names no framework; `grep -rn "@sentry/deno/import\|registerHooks" docs/ platform-includes/` returns nothing anywhere in the repo

DOC-F22Koa on Deno: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Koa on Deno page: instrumentation timing decides what you get

Description

No page covers Koa on Deno. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/koa/index.mdx:4 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.node, :5 fallbackGuide: javascript.node, and :12 is only <PlatformContent includePath="getting-started-node" />; grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/koa/ returns nothing, and docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 mentions no framework and no import hook.

Verified on Koa 3.2.1 with @koa/router 15.7.0 on Deno 2.9.0: npm install the app dependencies and @sentry/deno into a real node_modules, deno.json with {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"} and an imports map, an instrument.ts calling Sentry.init() from @sentry/deno that is evaluated before the Koa app module, and deno run --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys main.ts. Three shapes were exercised on top of that. (A) Bundle the server with esbuild and sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild (format: 'esm', external: ['@sentry/deno', '@sentry/server-utils'], plus a createRequire banner; both externals and the banner are load-bearing) and run the bundle. (B) Skip the bundler and start with deno run --import=./preload.ts main.ts, where preload.ts first registers a node:module load hook that returns .json URLs with format: "json" and then imports "@sentry/deno/import". (C) For database spans without either, import the Koa app module first, then register the hook in the entry body with await import("@sentry/deno/import"), then await import("mysql") and create the client with mysql.createConnection.

Works today:

  • Automatic route-error capture with mechanism auto.middleware.koa, in shapes A and B, with no app.on('error') of your own; in shape C, register app.on('error', err => Sentry.captureException(err)) yourself.
  • Parameterized names GET /users/:id with source route, http.route set and koa router spans, in shapes A and B, in both trace lifecycles.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql in shape C, on the request transaction.
  • Logs, carrying the request trace id.

Known limits:

  • With no build step and no hook, nothing Koa-specific is instrumented: transactions are GET /users/42 with source url, the streamed server span is GET with no http.route, there are no koa spans, and a thrown route error is not captured at all.
  • import "@sentry/deno/import" must not be a static import of the entry file. Deno instantiates the entry's whole static module graph, Koa included, before the first statement runs, so the hook registers after Koa is already in the module cache and never transforms it, and the same import kills the process anyway: Koa's statuses dependency does require('./codes.json'), and Deno compiles JSON as JavaScript while any module load hook is registered (denoland/deno#36240), so it aborts with SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':'. The JSON load hook in shape B is what makes the flag form survive.
  • Use mysql.createConnection, not mysql.createPool: pooled queries emit their db span as a root transaction of its own, on a different trace from the request.
  • @sentry/deno exports no setupKoaErrorHandler, so the unbundled fallback is the hand-written app.on('error') listener, whose events carry a manual mechanism.
  • debug: true prints "[instrumentation:koa] subscribing to channels" and "Integration installed: Koa" even when no hook is loaded and nothing is instrumented, so the debug log cannot be used to confirm the setup.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented: no http.client span and no sentry-trace/baggage, in both trace lifecycles. node:http/node:https requests are traced and propagated.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/koa.mdx with the setup, the three shapes, the database rule and the limits.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F22, cell M-koa-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/koa.mdx (new), with an "Instrumentation timing on Deno" section covering `--import` vs static import and the JSON load-hook workaround

A Koa-on-Deno page must exist and must document instrumentation timing, which is where every Koa capability succeeded or failed. It must say: (1) A static `import "@sentry/deno/import"` as the first import of the entry file does not work for Koa — Deno instantiates the whole module graph, including `koa`, before any module body executes, so the hook is registered after Koa is already cached and never transforms it; load it with the CLI flag instead (`deno run --import=./preload.ts main.ts`). (2) On Deno 2.9.0, registering any `Module.registerHooks` load hook breaks CJS `require('*.json')` (denoland/deno#36240), and Koa's `statuses` dependency does exactly that, so the preload must register a JSON-restoring load hook first (`registerHooks({ load(url, ctx, next) { const r = next(url, ctx); return url.endsWith('.json') ? { ...r, format: 'json' } : r; } })` or the `readFileSync` equivalent); with the flag plus that hook, route names, `http.route`, koa `router` spans and automatic error capture all work, in both lifecycles. (3) The bundler alternative was verified for the same set: esbuild with `sentryOrchestrionPlugin()` from `@sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild` (`format: 'esm'`, `external: ['@sentry/deno', '@sentry/server-utils']`, `createRequire` banner) produces `auto.middleware.koa` error events, `GET /users/:id` with source `route` and `router` spans with no runtime hook and no manual `app.on('error')` handler; `format: 'cjs'` and a bundle without the banner both fail, so the snippet has to be exact. So the route-name gap is a property of the plain unbundled setup, not of Koa on Deno. (4) For database spans without bundling, keep `import "@sentry/deno/import"` out of the static import list, register it in the entry body after Koa is imported (`await import('@sentry/deno/import')`), then dynamically import the driver and create the client with `mysql.createConnection` — a `createPool` query lands its db span on a separate trace instead of the request transaction. Isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs need no caveat. Two things must be said as warnings: `debug: true` reports Koa as instrumented even when it is not, and outgoing `fetch` is not instrumented — no `http.client` span, no `sentry-trace`/`baggage`, in both `static` and `stream` lifecycles — while `node:http`/`node:https` are traced normally. The only thing that restored fetch on this stack was a hand-written integration, so the page states the gap and names no remedy; SDK-side, the fetch integration that @sentry/cloudflare and @sentry/vercel-edge ship would close it for every Deno framework.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/koa/index.mdx:4 `sdk: sentry.javascript.node`, :5 `fallbackGuide: javascript.node`, :12 body is only `<PlatformContent includePath="getting-started-node" />`; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/koa/` returns nothing; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 never mentions Koa or any framework, and no page in the repo mentions `@sentry/deno/import` or `Module.registerHooks`

What ran: Koa 3.2.1 + @koa/router 15.7.0 on Deno 2.9.0 (aarch64-apple-darwin), @sentry/deno 10.67.0 from develop (worktree tarball). Cell record: M-koa-deno.

Add a Hapi on Deno page with the three verified wirings

Neither guide covers the pair: grep -rn -i hapi docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/ and grep -rn -i deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hapi/ both return nothing. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hapi/index.mdx:4-5 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.node with fallbackGuide: javascript.node and :12 is a bare getting-started-node include. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 covers error monitoring and tracing only, and never mentions node_modules, nodeModulesDir or the import hook.

Three shapes were verified.   • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented: no http.client span and no sentry-trace/baggage, with and without the hook and in both trace lifecycles. node:http/node:https requests are traced and propagated.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F23, cell M-hapi-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/hapi.mdx (new), covering the esbuild/orchestrion route, the attachHapiErrorHandler route and the import-hook + JSON-load-hook route side by side

A Hapi-on-Deno page must exist and must distinguish what needs the import hook from what does not. It must say: setup is `npm install @hapi/hapi` plus `@sentry/deno` into a real on-disk `node_modules` with `deno.json` `{"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}`, and `hapiIntegration()` is already a default integration, so nothing has to be added to `integrations`. Error capture does NOT require the `@sentry/deno/import` runtime hook: either bundle the app with esbuild and `sentryOrchestrionPlugin()` from `@sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild` (`bundle: true, platform: 'node', format: 'esm', external: ['@sentry/deno','@sentry/server-utils']`, `loader: {'.json': 'json'}`, plus a `createRequire` banner), which additionally yields parameterized route names and hapi/database spans, or call `attachHapiErrorHandler(server)` from `@sentry/server-utils` on the unbundled app — a shipped public export that works before or after `server.start()`. The Hapi guide's own step, `await Sentry.setupHapiErrorHandler(server)`, is not exported by `@sentry/deno` and throws there, so that include needs a Deno branch. Parameterized transaction names have two routes: the full one is `import "@sentry/deno/import"` plus a JSON load hook registered before the app (`Module.registerHooks({ load(url, ctx, next) { return url.endsWith('.json') ? { format: 'json', source: readFileSync(fileURLToPath(url), 'utf8'), shortCircuit: true } : next(url, ctx); } })`), which also brings the `router` child span, the `db` spans and `auto.function.hapi` error capture; the light one is a `server.ext('onPreHandler')` that renames the root span and then sets `Sentry.SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE` to `'route'`, which needs neither hook nor bundler but leaves the router and db spans out. Order the two hook registrations however you like; only their position before the app import matters. Isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs work with no caveat. Outgoing `fetch` is not instrumented: no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers, independent of the framework, of the hook and of `traceLifecycle`, while `node:http` requests are traced normally; nothing shipped restored it on this stack, so the page states it as a limit. SDK-side, `@sentry/deno` re-exporting the hapi error handler and shipping the fetch integration that @sentry/cloudflare already has would remove both rough edges.

Where the docs stand: `grep -rn -i hapi docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/` returns nothing (that directory holds only index.mdx and configuration/integrations/{breadcrumbs,contextlines}.mdx) and `grep -rn -i deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hapi/` returns nothing; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hapi/index.mdx:4-5 declares `sdk: sentry.javascript.node` / `fallbackGuide: javascript.node` with a bare `getting-started-node` body at :12; platform-includes/getting-started-use/javascript.hapi.mdx:17 and :42 still tell users to call `await Sentry.setupHapiErrorHandler(server)`; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 covers error monitoring and tracing only and never mentions `node_modules`, `nodeModulesDir`, or the `@sentry/deno/import` hook

What ran: @hapi/hapi 21.4.10 on Deno 2.9.0 (deno.json nodeModulesDir: "manual", npm node_modules on disk). Cell record: M-hapi-deno.

DOC-F24Oak on Deno: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add an Oak on Deno page: mount with Deno.serve(app.fetch)

Description

Oak appears nowhere in the docs: grep -rin '\boak\b' docs/ platform-includes/ and grep -rl '@oak/oak\|jsr:@oak' docs/ return nothing. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 names no framework and never mentions Deno.serve, only bare deno run at :100 and :119; the Deno guide directory holds just index.mdx and two integration pages.

Verified on Oak 17.2.0 (jsr:@oak/oak) and Deno 2.9.0: deno.json with {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"} and imports for @oak/oak and @sentry/deno, @sentry/deno present in a real node_modules, plus npm install path-to-regexp@6.3.0, which Oak needs to boot under manual nodeModulesDir. Put import "@sentry/deno/import"; as the first line of the instrument file, then Sentry.init(), and mount the app with Deno.serve(app.fetch). Name routes with a middleware registered before app.use(router.routes()) that reads ctx.matched?.at(-1)?.path and, inside try { await next(); } finally { ... }, calls Sentry.updateSpanName(span, `${ctx.request.method} ${route}`) and sets both Sentry.SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE to route and http.route to the route. A router.use((ctx, next) => { ...; return next(); }) registered before the routes is equivalent.

Works today:

  • Route errors, mechanism auto.http.deno, on the app.fetch path.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • GET /users/:id with source route and http.route set, via the naming middleware, on the success and the error path, in both trace lifecycles, and with prefixed routers (GET /api/users/:id).
  • Database spans for mysql through the import hook, with the driver imported dynamically after init.
  • Logs, correlated to the request trace.

Known limits:

  • app.listen() runs handlers outside the request's async context: no active span, errors are never captured, and tags and trace ids leak between requests. Nothing in user code repairs it, so always mount with Deno.serve(app.fetch).
  • Naming code placed after await next() instead of in a finally block silently skips every request whose handler throws, so those keep the unparameterized name.
  • Without the middleware, spans are named by HTTP method only (stream) or by the raw path with source url (static); there is no Oak integration in @sentry/deno.
  • Oak types app.fetch as a Cloudflare handler, so ctx.request.ip is empty on Deno. The SDK still records the client address, which it takes from the Deno.serve connection info.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented; node:http requests are.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/oak.mdx, leading with the mounting rule.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F24, cell M-oak-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/oak.mdx (new), with the `Deno.serve(app.fetch)` vs `app.listen()` warning as its first section

An Oak page must exist and must lead with the mounting shape, because the two Oak mounting styles are not equivalent for Sentry: mount with `Deno.serve(app.fetch)` and never with `app.listen()` — under `app.listen()` handlers run outside the request's async context, so there is no active span, errors are never captured at all and the isolation scope leaks between requests. With `Deno.serve(app.fetch)`, errors, isolation, incoming trace continuation, database spans and logs all work with no extra wiring. Two caveats must be documented. (1) Route names: Oak spans are named by HTTP method only until the app names them; register a middleware BEFORE `app.use(router.routes())` that reads `ctx.matched?.at(-1)?.path` and names the span inside `try { await next(); } finally { ... }`, calling `Sentry.updateSpanName(span, \`${ctx.request.method} ${route}\`)` and then setting `Sentry.SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE` to `'route'` and `http.route` to the route. The `finally` is the load-bearing part: the same code written after `await next()` never runs when the handler throws, so error responses keep the raw path. A router-level `router.use((ctx, next) => { ...; return next(); })` behaves identically, and both were verified in the `static` and `stream` lifecycles, including with prefixed routers. SDK-side, `setHttpServerSpanRouteAttribute` from `@sentry/server-utils` does exactly this job but is not re-exported from `@sentry/deno`, which is why the page has to spell the middleware out. (2) Outgoing requests: `@sentry/deno` does not instrument Deno's global `fetch` — no `http.client` span, no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` header, so the downstream service starts a new trace. The verified alternative is to make the call with `node:http` instead (`http.get(url, ...)` produced `http.client GET <url>` plus both headers, in both trace lifecycles); nothing shipped restores it for `fetch` itself on this stack. The page must also show the `deno.json` shape the run needed: `{"nodeModulesDir": "manual", "imports": {"@oak/oak": "jsr:@oak/oak@^17", "@sentry/deno": "npm:@sentry/deno"}}` with the SDK present in a real `node_modules`, and note that `ctx.request.ip` is empty because Oak types `app.fetch` as a Cloudflare handler.

Where the docs stand: `grep -rin '\boak\b' docs/ platform-includes/` and `grep -rl '@oak/oak\|jsr:@oak' docs/` return nothing; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 names no framework and never mentions `Deno.serve` (only bare `deno run` at :100 and :119); guides/deno/ contains only index.mdx and configuration/integrations/{breadcrumbs,contextlines}.mdx

What ran: Oak 17.2.0 (jsr:@oak/oak) on Deno 2.9.0 (aarch64-apple-darwin), @sentry/deno develop (10.67.0, worktree audit-runtime-docs). Cell record: M-oak-deno.

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Fresh 2 on Deno page: what the SDK does and does not instrument

Description

There is no Fresh page at all, and docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 names no framework; the Deno guide directory holds only index.mdx and configuration/integrations/{breadcrumbs,contextlines}.mdx, so the Cloudflare-style guides/*/frameworks/ layout has no Deno counterpart.

  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql@2, with the driver externalized as above.
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • Routes are not named automatically; a middleware that reads ctx.route and calls Sentry.updateSpanName on the root span is required, otherwise transactions carry the raw path with source url.
  • Per-request isolation is not reliable on Deno: awaiting a dynamic import() inside a request restores that request's store into the root async context, so tags, users, contexts and breadcrumbs set during one request appear on every later request. Mitigate with a first middleware that strips tags, contexts, extra, user, breadcrumbs and attachments off Sentry.getIsolationScope(). Do not use Sentry.withIsolationScope(new Sentry.Scope(), ...) for this: it cleans events but attributes each request's tags to the next transaction.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F25, cell M-fresh-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/fresh.mdx (new, following the guides/cloudflare/frameworks/*.mdx pattern), plus a new guides/deno/frameworks/index.mdx listing the supported Deno frameworks

A Fresh 2 page must exist and state that Fresh on Deno is viable but that nothing framework-specific is instrumented automatically — a hand-written middleware is required. It must document, in this order: (1) Setup — scaffold with `deno run -A jsr:@fresh/init@^2`, set `"nodeModulesDir": "manual"` in `deno.json`, keep `import "@sentry/deno/import"` as the first line of `instrument.ts` and `instrument.ts` as the first import of `main.ts`. (2) Errors — Fresh catches route errors itself and returns its own 500 page, so unhandled errors never reach Sentry on their own; the verified fix is `integrations: [Sentry.captureConsoleIntegration({ levels: ['error'], handled: false })]` in `Sentry.init`, which reports them as real exceptions carrying the request's trace id, in both `deno task dev` and the `deno serve _fresh/server.js` build. That integration works because Fresh's default error handler console.errors what it swallows, so it goes silent the moment the app registers its own `app.onError("*")` — as do a try/catch middleware and `setErrorInterceptor`. An app with its own handler must call `Sentry.captureException(ctx.error, { mechanism: { type: 'fresh.onError', handled: false } })` inside it; an app without one can use `setErrorInterceptor(app, ...)` from `jsr:@fresh/core@^2.3.3/internal` as the least intrusive alternative. (3) Route names — spans stay unparameterized unless the app renames them: register a middleware right after `staticFiles()` that reads `ctx.route` and calls `Sentry.updateSpanName` on the root span, which is the smallest way to get `sentry.segment.name.source: 'route'`. The page must also explain why Fresh's own route enrichment has no effect: the Deno SDK registers an OpenTelemetry TracerProvider but no ContextManager, so `trace.getActiveSpan()` from `@opentelemetry/api` returns `undefined` and the `http.route` Fresh sets there is dropped. Registering a ContextManager that bridges that key to the active Sentry root span makes Fresh name its own routes with no middleware at all, in dev and in the build, but it is a page of shim code, so the middleware is the shape to document and the bridge is worth naming as the reason a fix belongs in the SDK. (4) Isolation — per-request isolation is not reliable on Deno: awaiting a dynamic `import()` inside a request handler restores that request's AsyncLocalStorage store into the root context, permanently, so `Sentry.setTag`/`setUser`/`setContext`/`addBreadcrumb` from one request attach to every later request. This is not specific to Fresh, to route chunks or to the production build: it reproduces with zero Sentry, under both `Deno.serve` and `node:http`, and in `vite dev` as soon as app code awaits an `import()`; Fresh's build merely guarantees it, because each route chunk is loaded lazily on its first request. The verified mitigation is a first middleware that scrubs the inherited isolation scope (`Sentry.getIsolationScope()`, then clear tags, contexts, extra, user, breadcrumbs and attachments), and the page must warn against the obvious alternative: `Sentry.withIsolationScope(new Sentry.Scope(), ...)` cleans the events but then applies each request's tags to the following transaction. (5) Outgoing fetch — no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers for native `fetch`; `node:http`/`node:https` clients are traced, and nothing shipped closed the `fetch` gap on this stack, so the page states it as a limit. (6) Database spans — `@fresh/plugin-vite` sets `resolve.noExternal: true`, so every instrumented driver must be listed in BOTH `ssr: { external: [...] }` (for `deno task dev`) and `environments: { ssr: { build: { rollupOptions: { external: [...] } } } }` (for `deno task build`); `ssr.external` alone does not survive the build. Use `mysql@2`: `mysql2` dies on the JSON-require defect under the import hook. Incoming trace continuation and logs need no caveat.

Where the docs stand: No page mentions Fresh: `grep -rniE "\bfresh\b" docs/platforms/javascript/` returns only docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/agent-tracing/agents-sdk.mdx:43 ("a fresh conversation ID") and docs/platforms/javascript/common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:1339 ("a fresh scope"), and there is no docs/platforms/javascript/guides/fresh/ directory; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 is the entire Deno guide (plus configuration/integrations/{breadcrumbs,contextlines}.mdx) and names no framework — its only wiring is `import * as Sentry from "@sentry/deno"` + `Sentry.init` under "### Initialize the Sentry SDK" at :54, with the init call at :69, `--allow-net` at :100 and `--allow-read` at :119

DOC-F26NestJS on Deno: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a NestJS on Deno page: the boot workaround decides what you get

Description

No page covers NestJS on Deno. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nestjs/index.mdx:1-9 declares sdk: sentry.javascript.nestjs, fallbackGuide: javascript.node and category server-node, and grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nestjs/ returns zero hits. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 is a beta-flagged manual-setup page for @sentry/deno (Alert at :12-16) that names no framework.

Verified on NestJS 10 (@nestjs/core with @nestjs/platform-express, and re-run on @nestjs/platform-fastify) and @sentry/nestjs 10.67.0 on Deno 2.9.0: install the Sentry packages in a single npm install into a real on-disk node_modules, set deno.json to {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"} with experimentalDecorators and emitDecoratorMetadata in compilerOptions, keep the standard instrument file that calls Sentry.init({ dsn, tracesSampleRate: 1.0 }) from @sentry/nestjs first, and register SentryGlobalFilter as APP_FILTER alongside SentryModule.forRoot(). @sentry/deno is neither used nor needed. One more line is mandatory, or the app does not boot on Deno, and the choice decides how much you get. Either add a six-line Module.registerHooks({ load(url, context, nextLoad) { const result = nextLoad(url, context); return url.endsWith(".json") ? { ...result, format: "json" } : result; } }) from node:module in the instrument file, before the @sentry/nestjs import or right after Sentry.init() (both orders verified), and leave the SDK's diagnostics-channel injection on. Or turn the injection off with enableRuntimeChannelInjection: false in Sentry.init, the public option for it, which the Next.js-on-Deno run used on the same runtime.

Works today:

  • Handler errors, mechanism auto.http.nestjs.global_filter, linked to the request transaction.
  • With the JSON load hook and injection left on: GET /users/:id with source route and http.route set, plus express middleware, handler and function AppController.getUser spans, on @nestjs/platform-express and on @nestjs/platform-fastify, in both trace lifecycles.
  • Per-request isolation across concurrent requests, with distinct trace ids.
  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql2, which ships its own diagnostics channels, with no hook and no bundler plugin.
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • With injection off, transaction names are not parameterized: /users/42 reports GET /users/42 with source url, under span streaming the span is GET with no source attribute, and there are no express or nest middleware, handler or function spans.
  • With injection on and no JSON load hook, the app does not start: Sentry.init registers module hooks on Deno unconditionally, Deno then compiles every require('*.json') as JavaScript (denoland/deno#36240), and Nest fails with "No driver (HTTP) has been selected" on platform-express and SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':' on platform-fastify.
  • With injection off, only drivers that publish their own diagnostics channels are instrumented; anything that needs the injected channels stays dark.
  • Without SentryGlobalFilter as APP_FILTER, Nest swallows the error and only the failed transaction arrives. SentryModule.forRoot() registers APP_INTERCEPTOR only.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented: no http.client span and no sentry-trace/baggage headers, because that instrumentation listens on undici's diagnostics channels and Deno's native fetch publishes none. node:http requests are traced and propagated.
  • On Deno the SDK logs "@sentry/server-runtime-injection was bundled into your application" for an app that is not bundled, so that line is no signal of a misconfiguration.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/nestjs.mdx with the install, deno.json, init and filter wiring, both boot options with what each one costs, and a "Known limitations on Deno" section.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F26, cell M-nestjs-deno

Page: guides/deno/frameworks/nestjs.mdx (new), leading with the boot workaround and with an explicit "Known limitations on Deno" section

A NestJS-on-Deno page must exist. It must say NestJS 10 (`@nestjs/core` + `@nestjs/platform-express`) boots on Deno unchanged and that `@sentry/nestjs` works there, with these specifics: install the Sentry packages in a single `npm install` command into a real `node_modules` and set `deno.json` `{"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}` with the decorator compiler options. The first thing the page has to give the reader is the boot workaround, because the documented instrument file does not start on Deno: `Sentry.init` always installs its diagnostics-channel injection there, and with any module hook registered Deno compiles `require('*.json')` as JavaScript (denoland/deno#36240), which is fatal for Nest — express reports "No driver (HTTP) has been selected", fastify throws `SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':'`. Two levers exist and they are not equivalent. Registering a six-line compensating `Module.registerHooks({ load })` that restores `format: 'json'` keeps the injection on, and then Deno behaves like Node: `GET /users/:id` with source `route`, express/nest middleware, handler and function spans, and `auto.http.nestjs.global_filter` error events, on both HTTP adapters and in both trace lifecycles. Setting `enableRuntimeChannelInjection: false` also boots the app, and is the public option for what the run used, but it buys the plain shape: names stay `GET /users/42` with source `url`, there are no framework spans, and only drivers with their own diagnostics channels (mysql2 among them) are traced. The page must state both branches, since a reader who takes the simpler lever will otherwise read the missing route names as a bug. Error capture also needs `{ provide: APP_FILTER, useClass: SentryGlobalFilter }` next to `SentryModule.forRoot()`, which registers only `APP_INTERCEPTOR`; without it every handler throw is swallowed. Two facts belong in the limitations section: outgoing requests made with Deno's global `fetch` are not traced by `@sentry/node` or any framework SDK built on it, so there is no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` header (use a `node:http` client if you need propagation), and the SDK's "was bundled into your application" warning fires on Deno for apps that are not bundled. SDK-side, the Deno branch of `hasStableSyncModuleHooks()` returning true unconditionally is what turns a Deno runtime bug into an unbootable app, and a fetch integration in the Node SDK for runtimes without undici channels would close the second gap.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nestjs/index.mdx:1-9 declares `sdk: sentry.javascript.nestjs`, `fallbackGuide: javascript.node`, category `server-node`; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nestjs/` returns 0 hits; `grep -rn enableRuntimeChannelInjection docs/ platform-includes/` returns 0 hits, so the option the page needs is itself undocumented; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:1-230 is a beta-flagged manual-setup page for `@sentry/deno` (beta Alert at :12-16) that names no framework at all

What ran: NestJS 10 (@nestjs/core + @nestjs/platform-express, re-run on @nestjs/platform-fastify) with @sentry/nestjs 10.67.0 (develop / v11 line) on Deno 2.9.0 (aarch64-apple-darwin), npm compat, nodeModulesDir "manual". Cell record: M-nestjs-deno.

It must state that Next.js 15 App Router builds and serves on Deno 2.9 with `deno run -A node_modules/next/dist/bin/next build` and `... next start`, with `deno.json` `{"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}`, and then give the two deviations from the standard Next.js wiring, both of which are currently undocumented. (1) `enableRuntimeChannelInjection: false` must be set in `Sentry.init()` in `sentry.server.config.js` or every request 500s with `SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':'` — Deno cannot run the diagnostics-channel module hook because `require()` of a `.json` file throws (denoland/deno#36240; a pass-through `Module.registerHooks` with no Sentry in the process fails the same way). The page must document this option, which does not appear anywhere in the docs today. DOC-F28Nuxt on Deno: documented wrongP0proven

Correct the Nuxt Nitro preset page for Deno: keep the node-server preset

Description

docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7 claims that the SDK supports different Nitro deployment presets, "most of which work out of the box", and the 11-line file then documents only Cloudflare Pages at :9-11. The Nuxt guide has no Deno mention anywhere, so a reader picking the deno_server preset gets no warning. guides/nuxt/install/cli-import.mdx:37 gives the start command as node --import ..., which has no Deno equivalent.

Verified on Nuxt 4.5.2 with @sentry/nuxt 10.67.0 and Deno 2.9.0: do not set a Nitro preset. Build the default node-server output with npx nuxt build, keep sentry: { autoInjectServerSentry: 'top-level-import' } in nuxt.config.ts and a root sentry.server.config.ts that calls Sentry.init(), then run the output under Deno with cd .output/server && PORT=<port> deno run --allow-net --allow-read --allow-write --allow-env --allow-sys --allow-ffi --unstable-byonm ./index.mjs. Nitro's node-server entry uses node:http, which the SDK instruments on Deno, and the module injects the config import into the entry correctly with this preset. For database drivers add nitro: { externals: { inline: ['generate-function', 'named-placeholders'] } }. For outgoing requests, add winterCGFetchIntegration() from @sentry/vercel-edge to Sentry.init.

Works today:

  • Errors from nitro route handlers, with request data and source context.
  • Parameterized GET /api/users/:id with source route, in both trace lifecycles, and getActiveSpan() defined inside handlers. A control run of the same output under Node produced the same shape.
  • Database spans for mysql2, with the externals.inline entry above; on the node-server output they attach to ordinary route handlers with no code change.
  • http.client spans and sentry-trace/baggage on fetch and nitro's $fetch, with winterCGFetchIntegration(). It was measured on the deno_server build under the default stream lifecycle, and @sentry/vercel-edge is an extra package to install.
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • With nitro: { preset: 'deno_server' } there is no request instrumentation at all: no http.server spans, getActiveSpan() is undefined in handlers, every request shares one isolation scope and one trace id so tags and users leak, and incoming trace headers are ignored.
  • With deno_server and autoInjectServerSentry: 'top-level-import', the module writes the config import into a file literally named .output/server/deno.json start instead of the entry, so the wrapper entry that imports ./sentry.server.config.mjs before ./index.mjs has to be written by hand.
  • Under deno_server with traceLifecycle: 'static', an outgoing fetch outside an explicit Sentry.startSpan is dropped with no_parent_span, because there is no server span to parent it to.
  • Outgoing fetch is not instrumented on Deno without the integration above.
  • Route-name enrichment does not run on error responses, on Deno and on Node alike: a thrown handler reports GET /api/users/boom with source url.
  • The runtime debug line "Bundler plugin ran, injected libraries=[]" does not mean instrumentation is missing; that list is only filled during the build.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation were measured only on the deno_server build, where they fail. They are not yet measured on the node-server output.

What to change:

  • guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7: qualify the "work out of the box" claim; add a Deno section next to Cloudflare Pages at :9 with the run command and the deno_server warning.
  • guides/nuxt/install/cli-import.mdx:37: the node --import start command has no Deno equivalent; give the deno run form for the node-server output.
  • Cross-link a Deno framework page for Nuxt.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F28, cell M-nuxt-deno

Page: guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx — add a "Deno" section next to the existing "Cloudflare Pages" section at :9 and qualify the "work out of the box" claim at :7; give the Deno start command next to the Node-only one at guides/nuxt/install/cli-import.mdx:37; cross-link a new guides/deno/frameworks/nuxt.mdx with the full start command and integration snippet

The presets page must stop implying that every non-Cloudflare preset works out of the box and must add a Deno section saying the opposite for `deno_server`: with `nitro: { preset: 'deno_server' }` there is no request instrumentation — no `http.server` span, `getActiveSpan()` undefined in handlers, every request on one isolation scope and one trace id (so tags, users and breadcrumbs leak between requests), and incoming `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers not continued. The verified guidance is: do not set a Nitro preset on Deno. Keep the default `node-server` preset (`npx nuxt build`) and run the produced Node output under Deno with `cd .output/server && PORT=<port> deno run --allow-net --allow-read --allow-write --allow-env --allow-sys --allow-ffi --unstable-byonm ./index.mjs`, with `sentry: { autoInjectServerSentry: 'top-level-import' }`; nitro then serves over `node:http`, which the SDK instruments, and a control run of the identical output under Node produced the same telemetry, so Deno adds no gap of its own. Three further points belong on the page. (a) Database drivers need `nitro: { externals: { inline: ['generate-function', 'named-placeholders'] } }` in `nuxt.config.ts`, or mysql2 breaks in the nitro bundle; the debug line "Bundler plugin ran, injected libraries=[]" is a false negative and should not be read as missing instrumentation. (b) Outgoing requests made with `fetch` or nitro's `$fetch` get no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers, because Deno's fetch is not backed by `node:http`/undici — the verified remedy is `winterCGFetchIntegration()` from `@sentry/vercel-edge` in `sentry.server.config.ts`, which is an extra dependency; `@sentry/nuxt` already depends on `@sentry/cloudflare`, whose `fetchIntegration` is the same mechanism and worked on Deno in the Express and SvelteKit cells, so it is the cheaper candidate to verify next. Keep the default `stream` lifecycle: under `static` on `deno_server` a parentless fetch is dropped. (c) If `deno_server` is used anyway, the entry wrapper must be written by hand, because the module writes the config import into a file named `.output/server/deno.json start`. One non-Deno defect surfaced alongside: route-name enrichment does not run for thrown handlers on either runtime, so error transactions keep the raw path.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/features/nitro-deployment-presets.mdx:7 claims "The Sentry Nuxt SDK provides support for different Nitro deployment presets, most of which **work out of the box**" and the 11-line file then documents only Cloudflare Pages (:9-11) — the run contradicts this for the `deno_server` preset, which produced no request instrumentation at all; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/install/cli-import.mdx:37 prescribes `node --import ./.output/server/sentry.server.config.mjs .output/server/index.mjs`, a Node-only start command; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nuxt/` returns 0 hits and `grep -rniE "nuxt|nitro" docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/` returns 0 hits

Page: guides/sveltekit/manual-setup__v10.7.0.mdx:21-36 — correct the "Currently not supported" bullet at :32 and add Deno/adapter-node; restore an adapter-and-runtime compatibility section in guides/sveltekit/manual-setup.mdx (which currently has none); add guides/deno/frameworks/sveltekit.mdx with the run command, the `fetchIntegration` snippet and the mysql2 prewarm; and correct common/configuration/integrations/fetchIntegration.mdx:10

The adapter-compatibility note must be corrected to say that SvelteKit runs on Deno through adapter-node with no source changes: build with `vite build` under Node, then start the adapter output with `HOST=… PORT=… deno run --no-prompt --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys --allow-ffi build/index.js`. Errors, route names, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs all work unchanged. Two Deno-specific caveats must be added. (1) Outgoing HTTP: `@sentry/node`'s fetch instrumentation is inert on Deno because it listens on undici `diagnostics_channel` events and Deno's global `fetch` is native, so neither a global `fetch()` nor SvelteKit's `event.fetch` produces an `http.client` span or carries `sentry-trace`/`baggage`. Deno has an open bug (denoland/deno#36240) where registering any module hook makes `require('*.json')` compile as JavaScript, so a driver that reads its own `package.json` at runtime throws `SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':'` on first use; the verified fix is to put that JSON in the CJS require cache before `Sentry.init` runs — `createRequire(import.meta.url)('mysql2/package.json')` either in a preload file started with `deno run --preload ./prewarm-json.mjs`, or as the first two lines of `src/hooks.server.js` above the `@sentry/sveltekit` import. Both give a `db` span parented under the SvelteKit `http.server` span in both trace lifecycles, so mysql2 is usable, not blocked. The page should also say that the "@sentry/server-runtime-injection was bundled into your application" warning can appear on Deno while everything works. (3) The fetchIntegration reference page still states the integration only works inside Cloudflare Workers, which this run disproves, so it needs correcting too.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/sveltekit/manual-setup__v10.7.0.mdx:21-36 ("Notes on SvelteKit adapter compatibility") has the "Currently not supported" heading at :31 and the bullet at :32 — "Non-Node.js server runtimes, such as Vercel's edge runtime, are not yet supported" — which reads as excluding Deno, while :28 lists Adapter-node as fully supported; the run contradicts the exclusion (the adapter-node production build ran under Deno with errors, route names, isolation, incoming trace, db spans and logs all working). The current docs/platforms/javascript/guides/sveltekit/manual-setup.mdx has no such expandable at all (its body is only `sentry-init/manual-setup-callout` + `getting-started-complete`, :1-9), so the v11 line now makes no runtime statement whatsoever; `grep -rni deno docs/platforms/javascript/guides/sveltekit/` returns 0 hits; common/configuration/integrations/fetchIntegration.mdx:10 still says the integration "only works inside the Cloudflare Workers runtime"

Add a React Router on Deno page with the Deno entry.server and node:http server entry

Description

docs/platforms/javascript/guides/react-router/ contains only index.mdx, manual-setup.mdx and profiling/, and grep -rni deno over it returns nothing; the reverse grep over guides/deno/ also returns nothing. The guide is Node-shaped throughout: index.mdx:9 categorises it server-node, manual-setup.mdx:400-401 gives the only start commands and both are Node ones, and the "Limited Node support for OpenTelemetry auto-instrumentation" Expandable at :186-197 is the closest the guide comes to a runtime statement.

Verified on React Router 7.18.3 in framework mode with @sentry/react-router 10.67.0 on Deno 2.9.0: npm install the app packages and the Sentry packages into a real node_modules, deno.json with {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}. app/entry.server.tsx must use the web-stream renderer, renderToReadableStream from react-dom/server, because Deno resolves react-dom/server through the deno export condition and there is no renderToPipeableStream; wrap it with wrapSentryHandleRequest and export handleError = createSentryHandleError({ logErrors: true }). The server entry is a hand-written node:http adapter whose first line is import './instrument.server.mjs'; and which serves createRequestHandler(build, 'production') over http.createServer; build with npx react-router build and start it with PORT=<port> deno run --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --allow-sys server.mjs. Add the Vite plugin as import { sentryReactRouter } from '@sentry/react-router/vite'. Use mysql@2, not mysql2.

Works today:

  • Loader errors, mechanism react-router, surfaced by the handleError export.
  • GET /users/:id with op http.server and source route.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql@2 with no extra instrumentation path: the SDK subscribes to the driver's diagnostics channel eagerly on Deno. The connection must be created inside the loader.
  • Outgoing fetch(), with fetchIntegration imported from @sentry/cloudflare and registered in instrument.server.mjs behind a Deno-only guard, so a Node build does not emit a duplicate http.client span.
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • The two start paths the guide prescribes do not work on Deno: react-router-serve goes through the renderToPipeableStream path Deno cannot load, and NODE_OPTIONS='--import ./instrument.server.mjs' is ignored by Deno. A bare Deno.serve handler produces neither the http.server span nor the isolation scope, so the node:http server entry above is the load-bearing piece.
  • mysql2 cannot be loaded once Sentry is initialized: its require('../package.json') throws SyntaxError: Unexpected token ':' through the SDK's runtime require hook. Plain Deno imports it fine.
  • With mysql@2, a module-scope pool detaches its query: the db span arrives as its own root transaction on a different trace while the request transaction has no children.
  • Without the fetch integration above, an outgoing fetch() gets no http.client span and no sentry-trace/baggage headers, so the trace ends at the server. node:http requests are traced and propagated.
  • Error events carry the raw path in their transaction field while the transaction itself carries the route pattern.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/react-router.mdx and cross-link it from guides/react-router/manual-setup.mdx near :400-401 and from the Node-support Expandable at :186-197.

A React Router (framework mode) on Deno page must exist. It must say up front that the app runs on Deno but NOT with the `entry.server.tsx` the current guide shows, and give the working entry and install shape (`npm install` of react-router, the Sentry packages and the driver into a real `node_modules`). The second thing it must give is the server entry, because that is what every span depends on: a hand-written `node:http` adapter whose first line imports `instrument.server.mjs` and which serves `createRequestHandler(build, 'production')` through `http.createServer`. Both start paths the guide prescribes are unusable here — `react-router-serve` needs `renderToPipeableStream`, which Deno's `react-dom/server` does not have, and Deno ignores `NODE_OPTIONS` — and a bare `Deno.serve` handler gets neither the `http.server` span nor the isolation scope. The Vite plugin is imported from `@sentry/react-router/vite`, the subpath it moved to. Two Deno-specific behaviours then have to be documented. (1) Databases: no extra instrumentation path is needed, because the SDK subscribes to the driver's diagnostics channel eagerly on Deno, but the driver choice and the connection placement decide whether the span is usable. `mysql2` cannot be loaded at all once Sentry is initialized — its `require('../package.json')` dies in the SDK's runtime require hook — and with `mysql@2` a module-scope pool emits the query as its own root transaction on a separate trace, so the connection has to be created inside the loader for the span to nest under the request. The verified remedy is one shipped line: `fetchIntegration` from `@sentry/cloudflare` in `instrument.server.mjs`, guarded to Deno so a Node build does not double-instrument the same call. Errors, route names, isolation, incoming trace continuation and logs all work; one wart to mention is that error events carry the raw path in `transaction` while the transaction carries the route pattern.

Deno has no node --import, so add a two-line .output/server/deno-entry.mjs that imports ./instrument.server.mjs then ./index.mjs, and start it with PORT=<port> deno run --allow-net ... deno-entry.mjs. Build with vite 8.x and the pristine config: vite 7.3.2 with nitro 3.0.260903-beta crashes rollup with Cannot read properties of null (reading 'getVariableForExportName') whether or not Sentry is involved, and a pre-plugin that externalises every @sentry/* import gets past that crash but silently removes the route parametrization. Confirm the injection landed before deploying with grep -l 'users/$userId' .output/server/_libs/sentry__tanstackstart-react.mjs.

Works today:

  • Server route errors, mechanism auto.middleware.tanstackstart.request.
  • Parameterized server route names (GET /users/$userId, source route), with noExternals set, in both trace lifecycles. A Node control run of the same output produced the same names.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql, injected at build time by the Sentry Vite plugin.
  • Logs.

Known limits:

  • If the build externalizes @sentry/tanstackstart-react, transaction names stay raw, for example GET /users/123 with source url. This is a build-config effect, not a Deno one: the same build produces url-source names under Node, and the SDK emits no warning when the route-pattern substitution did not happen.
  • The build is version-sensitive: vite 7.3.2 with nitro 3.0.260903-beta fails in rollup with or without Sentry, and the externalising pre-plugin that works around it costs the route names.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented on Deno: no http.client span and no sentry-trace/baggage headers.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/tanstack-start.mdx.

A TanStack Start on Deno page must exist. It must say the Nitro `node` output runs under Deno and that errors, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, database spans and logs all work, and it must replace the Node-only `node --import …` start command at :354-358 with the Deno equivalent. Two build-shape facts must be documented because neither is discoverable. (1) Server route parametrization (`GET /users/$userId` rather than `GET /users/123`) requires `@sentry/tanstackstart-react` to be bundled into the Nitro server build, because the Vite plugin injects the route patterns by transforming the SDK's own source; if the build externalizes the SDK the names stay raw. The verified fix is `nitro({ noExternals: ['@sentry/tanstackstart-react'] })` in `vite.config.ts`, alongside `tanstackStart()` and `sentryTanstackStart()`. (2) On Deno the server SDK does not instrument outgoing `fetch()` — no `http.client` span and no `sentry-trace`/`baggage` headers — so the trace stops at the server boundary. DOC-F33Remix on Deno: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Remix on Deno page covering installGlobals and the build-time setup

Description

The Remix guide names no runtime other than Node: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/remix/index.mdx:4-9 scopes the guide with Node categories and :12-16 carries only the Remix 3 alert, while platform-includes/getting-started-prerequisites/javascript.remix.mdx:1-7 requires just Remix 2.0.0+. Greps for deno, installGlobals and enableRuntimeChannelInjection over docs/ and platform-includes/ return zero; orchestrion returns two hits, both on common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx (:689 and :1297), so the option and the plugin are documented nowhere outside the migration page.

Verified on Remix 2.17.5 with the Remix Vite plugin and a custom node:http server on Deno 2.9.0: install with npm under Node using --legacy-peer-deps, set deno.json to {"nodeModulesDir": "manual"}, add sentryRemixVitePlugin(), imported from @sentry/remix/vite, next to remix() in vite.config.ts, export handleError = Sentry.sentryHandleError from app/entry.server.tsx, and import instrument.server.mjs as the first line of the server entry with Sentry.instrumentBuild(build). Call installGlobals() from @remix-run/node at the top of app/entry.server.tsx, which is what remix reveal generates: it replaces Deno's native fetch with the node:http-backed polyfill, and outgoing requests are then traced. For database spans there are two shapes. Either import the driver before instrument.server.mjs, which needs no instrumentation path at all because mysql2 3.20+ publishes its own diagnostics channels; or set enableRuntimeChannelInjection: false and bundle the server entry with sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild, with @remix-run/server-runtime bundled, @sentry/* and mysql2 external, and a createRequire banner on the ESM output.

Works today:

  • Loader errors, mechanism auto.function.remix.server.
  • GET users/:id with source route, plus nested loader spans.
  • Per-request isolation and incoming trace continuation.
  • Outgoing http.client spans with trace headers, after installGlobals(), in both trace lifecycles.
  • Database spans for mysql2, through either shape above, and logs.

Known limits:

  • Without installGlobals(), outgoing fetch() produces no span and no sentry-trace/baggage headers. node:http and undici clients are traced without it.
  • installGlobals() called from the server entry must run after import './instrument.server.mjs'. Calling it before Sentry.init() silently drops every transaction and span envelope for the whole process while errors keep arriving. In app/entry.server.tsx, the position remix reveal generates, the ordering takes care of itself.
  • Replacing the polyfill with a hand-written fetch integration is not a substitute: it propagates the headers but suppresses every server transaction and span for the process.
  • The esbuild bundle needs the createRequire banner; without it the server dies with Dynamic require of "@sentry/server-utils" is not supported. Bundling @sentry/* instead of leaving it external fails the same way.
  • Turning the runtime hook off without the bundler plugin silently removes every server span, and leaving it on makes Deno abort at startup on a CommonJS dependency that does require('*.json').
  • Express cannot be used as the Remix server on Deno; it fails at import.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/frameworks/remix.mdx and link it from guides/remix/frameworks/index.mdx, which today lists only the Cloudflare pages.
  • Name the supported runtimes in guides/remix/index.mdx:4-9 and in the prerequisites include, and document enableRuntimeChannelInjection and the orchestrion build plugin outside the migration page.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F33, cell M-remix-deno

Install with npm under Node using --legacy-peer-deps and set deno.json nodeModulesDir to manual; `deno add` does not resolve the transitive @sentry packages. Import the Vite plugin from `@sentry/remix/vite`, the subpath it now lives at. Call `installGlobals()` from `@remix-run/node` at the top of `app/entry.server.tsx`, or `fetch` in loaders and actions produces no `http.client` span and no trace headers, because Deno's native fetch is not instrumentable while the Remix polyfill is backed by `node:http`, which the Node SDK does instrument on Deno. The page must carry the ordering rule with it: if the call is made from the server entry instead, it has to come after `import './instrument.server.mjs'` — before `Sentry.init()` it silently drops every transaction and span envelope for the whole process while errors still arrive — and it must warn against the obvious alternative of a hand-written fetch integration, which propagates headers but suppresses all server spans. With this wiring, errors, parameterized route names, isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans, db spans and logs all work.

Proposed issue new-page

Add a SolidStart on Deno install page and record its instrumentation limits

Description

grep -rni deno over docs/platforms/javascript/guides/solidstart/ returns zero, index.mdx:1-7 names no server runtime, and there is no platform-includes/getting-started-prerequisites/javascript.solidstart.mdx. The tracing verification step at index.mdx:444-470 tells the reader to create src/routes/sentry-test.tsx, throw from it and expect an issue, which never arrives on SolidStart 2. guides/deno/index.mdx:1-18 covers only the Deno SDK, not a node-server Nitro build run under Deno.

Verified on SolidStart 2.0.2 with Nitro 3 beta, Vite 8 and @sentry/solidstart 10.67.0: install with npm and --legacy-peer-deps, and put solidStart({ appRoot: './src' }), sentrySolidStart() imported from @sentry/solidstart/vite, and nitro({ serverDir: './server' }) in vite.config.ts. Initialise in a Nitro startup plugin (server/plugins/sentry.ts) that calls Sentry.init(); no --import preload and no instrument file are needed, because the Sentry Vite plugin bakes the library instrumentation into the bundle. Build with NODE_ENV=production npx vite build on Node — the plugin skips its instrumentation pass in development, and that pass is what produces the database spans — then run the node-server output with PORT=<port> HOST=127.0.0.1 deno run -A .output/server/index.mjs on Deno 2.9.0. For error capture, register a try/catch middleware as the first entry of the array form of createMiddleware and call Sentry.captureException there.

Works today:

  • Incoming sentry-trace/baggage continuation.
  • Database spans for mysql2, injected at build time by the Sentry Vite plugin.
  • Logs.
  • Errors from API routes, with the middleware above; errors thrown inside 'use server' actions are already captured by the shipped withServerActionInstrumentation wrapper, mechanism auto.function.solidstart.
  • Parameterized names, with setHttpServerSpanRouteAttribute from @sentry/server-utils: an onBeforeResponse middleware that rebuilds the pattern from event.params covers API routes, and a useCurrentMatches() call in the Router root covers SSR pages. Verified in both trace lifecycles.
  • Outgoing fetch(), with winterCGFetchIntegration() from @sentry/vercel-edge in the Nitro startup plugin, gated on Deno so a Node build does not get duplicate spans. It bundles cleanly into the node-server output and is an extra install.

Known limits:

  • Unhandled errors from API routes are not captured on their own: SolidStart answers 500 and no event is sent, on Deno and on Node alike, so the guide's own verification snippet reports nothing. The object form of createMiddleware cannot fix this, because neither of its lifecycles wraps the handler; the array form is required, with the existing sentryBeforeResponseMiddleware() spread in through a nested object-form call.
  • The Nitro error hook cannot help either: SolidStart builds its own inner H3 instance whose default onError turns the throw into a 500, so @sentry/nitro's error hook never fires and adding @sentry/nitro as a module changes nothing.
  • The SDK does not name server transactions: SolidStart mounts one catch-all Nitro route, so without the middleware above names arrive as the raw path with source url. setHttpServerSpanRouteAttribute is not re-exported from @sentry/solidstart, so the app has to depend on @sentry/server-utils directly, and only the segment span is renamed; the inner non-segment http.server spans stay GET under the stream lifecycle.
  • Scope data set during one request is inherited by later requests in the process: the first setTag lands on the shared default isolation scope. Node with the same build is clean. The mechanism was not established for this stack.
  • Outgoing fetch() is not instrumented without the integration above; node:http/node:https clients produce http.client spans with headers on Deno either way.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/solidstart/install/deno.mdx, correct the verification step at index.mdx:444-470, and add the two missing platform-includes.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F34, cell M-solidstart-deno

Four findings follow. (1) Unhandled errors from API routes are not captured, on Deno and on Node alike, which is why the guide's own verification step at index.mdx:444-470 tells readers to expect an issue that never arrives. The fix is a try/catch middleware as the first entry of the ARRAY form of `createMiddleware`, rethrowing after `Sentry.captureException`, with `sentryBeforeResponseMiddleware()` spread in through a nested object-form call; the object form cannot do it, because neither of its lifecycles wraps the handler, and the Nitro `error` hook cannot either, because SolidStart's inner H3 instance already turned the throw into a 500. (3) Outgoing fetch produces no span and no trace headers, because the Node SDK relies on undici channels that Deno's fetch never emits; `winterCGFetchIntegration()` from `@sentry/vercel-edge`, added in the Nitro startup plugin behind a Deno guard, restores both and bundles cleanly into the node-server output. (4) Isolation is only partial: the first `setTag` of a request lands on the shared default isolation scope and later requests inherit it, while Node with the same build is clean. The cause was not established for this stack, so the page should state the symptom and not attribute it. Database spans, logs and incoming trace continuation work unmodified. Install needs npm with --legacy-peer-deps for the nitro beta. SDK-side, `@sentry/solidstart` hooking SolidStart's own matcher would remove the route-name middleware entirely.

Add an Elysia on Cloudflare Workers page and drop the Bun/Node-only runtime claim

Description

No page mentions Elysia on Cloudflare Workers. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/elysia/index.mdx:25 lists the prerequisites as "Bun or Node.js 18+ (with @elysiajs/node adapter)" and the install tabs at :55-60 plus the config snippet at :82 cover Bun and Node only. platform-includes/performance/what-instrumentation-provides/javascript.elysia.mdx:3-5 promises a span for every Elysia lifecycle phase (request_handler.elysia for Handle, middleware.elysia for the rest) with no runtime qualifier, and :9-12 has a "Runtime Behavior" section covering exactly two runtimes. Verified on the develop line: Elysia 1.4.30 on workerd with compatibility_date: "2026-05-01" and compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat"]. Install @sentry/cloudflare @sentry/elysia elysia. Construct the app as withElysia(new Elysia({ aot: false })) before any route is registered, and export Sentry.withSentry((env) => ({ dsn: env.SENTRY_DSN, tracesSampleRate: 1.0 }), { fetch: request => app.fetch(request) }). Do not call init from @sentry/elysia on Workers: its sdk.js imports node:os and @sentry/bun, so the Worker fails to build; Sentry.withSentry from @sentry/cloudflare is what supplies the client, and withElysia is the only part of @sentry/elysia used. Ahead-of-time compilation must be off: with the default, workerd refuses to start the Worker because Elysia's generated handler runs new Function and touches randomness in global scope. For database spans, build with Vite: plugins: [cloudflare(), sentryCloudflareVitePlugin()] from @sentry/cloudflare/vite, then vite build and point wrangler at the emitted config.

Works today:

  • Unhandled errors via Elysia's global error hook, parameterized route names, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with headers, Sentry.logger logs, and db spans through the Vite build.

Known limits:

  • A wrangler-bundled Worker emits no database spans.
  • No Elysia lifecycle spans on Workers: every transaction comes back with spans: [], because the app.trace() handler that creates them depends on rootSpanForRequest, which only the Bun branch of app.wrap populates.
  • withElysia must wrap the instance before routes are registered, or only the error path gets the route name.
  • mysql2 on workerd needs disableEval: true in the connection options, and the pool must be created at module scope.
