DOC-F38Fastify on Cloudflare: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue limitation-note

Note that Fastify does not run on Cloudflare Workers

Description

Neither guide says anything about this combination. grep -rni "cloudflare|workerd" docs/platforms/javascript/guides/fastify/ returns zero hits, docs/platforms/javascript/guides/fastify/index.mdx:4-9 declares the guide Node-only, and the Cloudflare framework list at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 names no Node HTTP framework at all. A reader gets no signal either way and spends the time finding out.

The audit ran Fastify 5.12.3 on workerd via wrangler dev with compatibility_date: "2026-05-01" and compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat", "enable_nodejs_http_server_modules"], serving through httpServerHandler from cloudflare:node. No wiring made Fastify serve a request, so there is no setup to document and no Sentry capability could be exercised. A control test with a plain node:http server behind the same httpServerHandler config did serve requests, so the Cloudflare half of the shape is fine.

  • Works today: nothing; Fastify never serves a request on Workers.

Known limits:

  • Constructing the app at module scope throws "Disallowed operation called within global scope", because Fastify's boot generates random values and schedules work.
  • Constructing it lazily on the first request then fails when any route is registered, because Fastify's router compiles matchers with new Function and Workers forbid runtime code generation, with no compatibility flag that lifts it.
  • Fastify 4 behaves the same way, so downgrading does not help.
  • Declaring a route schema is not an escape either: ajv (validation) and fast-json-stringify (response serialization) compile with new Function and hit the same wall.

What to change:

  • Add a "Node HTTP frameworks on Workers" note near Known Limitations at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:368, stating that Fastify does not run on Cloudflare Workers and why, and that the Cloudflare half of the shape is not the problem: enable_nodejs_http_server_modules plus httpServerHandler serve requests fine, it is the frameworks that cannot be constructed at module scope.
  • Add a one-line runtime note to docs/platforms/javascript/guides/fastify/index.mdx, scoped to Workers, making clear Fastify on Node.js is unaffected.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F38, cell M-fastify-cf

Page: Add a 'Node HTTP frameworks on Workers' section to guides/cloudflare/index.mdx near Known Limitations at :368 naming Fastify as not viable, plus a one-line runtime note in guides/fastify/index.mdx.

The docs are silent, and that silence leads users into a dead end. A page must state that Fastify does not run on Cloudflare Workers behind httpServerHandler, and why: constructing the app at module scope throws a disallowed-global-scope error because Fastify's boot generates random values and schedules work, and lazy construction on first request then hits a second workerd restriction that blocks serving, the ban on runtime code generation that Fastify's router, and ajv and fast-json-stringify behind any route schema, all depend on. No Sentry capability could be exercised. The note should also say what is not at fault: the node:http control behind the same httpServerHandler config served 200s, so the Cloudflare entrypoint works and it is the Node frameworks that cannot be constructed at module scope. The statement must be scoped to Workers; Fastify on Node is unaffected.

Koa 3 with @koa/router on workerd, compatibility_date: "2026-05-01", compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat", "enable_nodejs_http_server_modules"], served through httpServerHandler from cloudflare:node and wrapped with Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare. That yields a captured error and a route-shaped name with source custom rather than route.

Works today:

Known limits:

  • Without the Vite build there is no Koa instrumentation at all: unless you add the two lines above, errors are swallowed with no event and the transaction is named from the raw URL (GET /users/42, source url).
  • The manual rename gives source custom, not route, and no database spans exist on a plain wrangler build.
  • Creating the Koa app at module scope makes the Worker fail to start with "Disallowed operation called within global scope".
  • mysql2 on workerd needs disableEval: true in the connection options.

What to change:

  • Add the module-scope rule as a shared note on guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx.
  • Include the plain-wrangler fallback on the new page, so readers who cannot build with Vite still get errors and a usable transaction name.

A page must say that Koa 3 with @koa/router runs unchanged on workerd once the Node HTTP server flag is set and the server is served through httpServerHandler. DOC-F40Hapi on Cloudflare: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Hapi on Cloudflare Workers page and document build-time instrumentation

Description

Nothing documents Hapi on Workers. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/hapi/index.mdx:4-5 sets sdk: sentry.javascript.node with fallbackGuide: javascript.node and its whole body is one include at :12. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/ holds no hapi.mdx and the framework list at guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 omits Hapi. Separately, packages/server-utils/package.json:30-62 exports seven orchestrion entry points (./orchestrion/config plus six bundler entries: vite, rollup, webpack, webpack-loader, esbuild and bun), and the only mention of any of them in the docs is one line of the v10-to-v11 migration guide at common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:1297; no how-to page explains them.

Verified on the develop line: @hapi/hapi 21 on workerd with compatibility_date: "2026-05-01" and compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat", "enable_nodejs_http_server_modules"], served through httpServerHandler from cloudflare:node and wrapped with Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare. Build-time instrumentation is what makes the framework visible, by either of two public routes: sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() from @sentry/cloudflare/vite in a Vite build, or sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild when you pre-bundle the Worker yourself and point wrangler's main at the output (an esbuild banner that backs require with statically imported node: builtins is needed, or workerd fails at startup on node:async_hooks). With build-time instrumentation on, register routes and call server.start() inside the first fetch, never at module scope: the injected wrapper around Server.prototype.route starts a span, and the isolate then fails to boot with "Disallowed operation called within global scope". hapiIntegration() is not in the Cloudflare default integrations and is not re-exported by @sentry/cloudflare, so add it by hand from @sentry/server-utils.

Works today:

  • Parameterized transaction names (GET /users/{id}, source route) with nested router and extension spans, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, db spans from a mysql query, Sentry.logger logs, all on a build-time-instrumented bundle.
  • Route handler errors, after adding an onRequest extension that drops the premature close event: server.ext('onRequest', (request, h) => { const res = request.raw.res; const on = res.on.bind(res); res.on = (e, l) => (e === 'close' ? res : on(e, l)); return h.continue; }). With that in place hapiIntegration() captures the throw itself, with the transaction name and mechanism auto.function.hapi.
  • On a plain wrangler build, parameterized names from a four-line onPreResponse extension: Sentry.updateSpanName(root, `${request.method.toUpperCase()} ${request.route.path}`) plus root.setAttribute(Sentry.SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE, 'route') on Sentry.getRootSpan(Sentry.getActiveSpan()), all exports of @sentry/cloudflare.

Known limits:

  • Without the onRequest fix, nothing is captured: on workerd the node:http ServerResponse emits close before finish with writableEnded still false, hapi reads that as a client disconnect, answers 499 and never fires its request/error event. A server.ext('onPreResponse') that calls Sentry.captureException when request.response.isBoom is the fallback on builds without the instrumentation.
  • A plain wrangler build emits no database spans and no router or extension spans, and names the transaction from the raw URL unless you add the onPreResponse rename; wrangler's own bundler takes no plugins.
  • mysql 2.x works unchanged; mysql2 needs disableEval: true in the connection options.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hapi.mdx with the wiring above and link it from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23.
  • Add guides/cloudflare/features/build-time-instrumentation.mdx covering sentryOrchestrionPlugin for esbuild, rollup and webpack, and link it from the Vite plugin page.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F40, cell M-hapi-cf

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hapi.mdx (new), linked from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23, plus a new shared page guides/cloudflare/features/build-time-instrumentation.mdx covering sentryOrchestrionPlugin for non-Vite bundlers, which is exported but documented nowhere outside one migration-guide line.

A page must say that Hapi 21 runs on Workers behind the node:http shim, and document one hard defect plus two build shapes. On workerd the response emits close before finish with writableEnded still false, hapi reads that as a client disconnect, turns every response into a 499, and its request and error events never fire, so hapiIntegration() captures nothing on its own; it is also absent from the Cloudflare default integrations and not re-exported by @sentry/cloudflare, so it has to be imported from @sentry/server-utils. Show the app-side fix, an onRequest extension that drops the premature close before hapi's transmit sees it, which restores capture, the transaction name and the auto.function.hapi mechanism, and the onPreResponse isBoom fallback for builds with no instrumentation. Parameterized transaction names, router and extension spans and database spans all require build-time instrumentation, either the Cloudflare Vite plugin or the orchestrion plugin for esbuild, rollup or webpack wired into wrangler's build.command; a plain wrangler build still reaches source route with a four-line onPreResponse rename using updateSpanName and SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE from @sentry/cloudflare. The page must also state the module-scope rule: with build-time instrumentation on, routes and server.start() belong inside the first fetch. Isolation, incoming trace, outgoing fetch and logs work unaided. mysql 2.x runs unchanged, mysql2 needs disableEval: true.

Where the docs stand: guides/hapi/index.mdx:4-5 and :12 (Node fallback, body is one include); guides/cloudflare/frameworks/ has no hapi.mdx; guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 (no Hapi); sentryOrchestrionPlugin and @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion appear exactly once in the docs, at common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:1297, and nowhere as a how-to, although packages/server-utils/package.json:30-62 exports seven orchestrion entry points.

What ran: Hapi (@hapi/hapi 21) on cloudflare (workerd 1.20260903.1 via wrangler 4.129.0, compatibility_date 2026-05-01). Cell record: M-hapi-cf.

Add an Oak on Cloudflare Workers page covering the JSR install and withSentry wiring

Run with npx wrangler dev; no bundler plugin is required.

Known limits:

One Oak middleware, given below, restores parameterized names with source route in both lifecycles.
  • The server span is reported with status ok even when the handler threw.
  • mysql 2.x works with no configuration; mysql2 emits spans but its query fails on workerd unless you pass disableEval: true in the connection options, which this cell did not exercise.

  • Add Oak to the framework list at guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23.
  • Show the route-name middleware on that page: registered before router.routes(), it reads ctx.matched inside a try/finally (so it also runs when a handler throws) and calls Sentry.updateSpanName(root, routeName), root.setAttribute(Sentry.SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE, 'route') and root.setAttribute('http.route', pattern) on Sentry.getRootSpan(Sentry.getActiveSpan()), plus Sentry.getIsolationScope().setTransactionName(routeName) so error events carry the route too, all exports of @sentry/cloudflare. Use the exported SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE constant, not the legacy string 'sentry.source', which the SDK ignores; with the constant both trace lifecycles report source route.

DOC-F42Fresh on Cloudflare: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a Fresh 2 on Cloudflare Workers page with the esbuild orchestrion build step

Description

Fresh is absent from the docs. There is no guides/fresh/ directory, and the only "fresh" hits under docs/platforms/javascript/ are the ordinary English word (for example guides/cloudflare/agent-tracing/agents-sdk.mdx:43). The Cloudflare framework list at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 names Astro, Hono, Hydrogen, Next.js, Nuxt, Remix, SvelteKit and TanStack Start and nothing else.

Verified on the develop line: Fresh 2 on workerd with compatibility_date: "2026-05-01" and compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat"]. Fresh's own Vite plugin emits _fresh/server.js whose default export is already { fetch }, so no Cloudflare adapter is needed. Keep @sentry/cloudflare and any database driver out of the Fresh SSR bundle via environments.ssr.build.rollupOptions.external, then write a separate wrangler entry that wraps Fresh's server with Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare. For database spans, bundle that wrangler entry yourself with esbuild plus sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild, and point wrangler's main at the output. That esbuild build needs a banner defining a globalThis.require backed by static node:* imports, or workerd dies at the first query with "Dynamic require of 'diagnostics_channel' is not supported".

Route names take one middleware in main.ts, registered before app.fsRoutes(): in a finally around ctx.next(), call rootSpan.updateName(`${ctx.req.method} ${ctx.route}`), then rootSpan.setAttribute("http.route", ctx.route) and rootSpan.setAttribute(Sentry.SENTRY_SEGMENT_NAME_SOURCE, "route"), on Sentry.getRootSpan(Sentry.getActiveSpan()) from @sentry/cloudflare. The order matters: the source has to be set after the rename, because both span.updateName() and Sentry.updateSpanName() mark the name custom. The finally matters too, because Fresh's error boundary sits outside user middleware, so a throwing route would otherwise keep the unparameterized name.

Works today:

  • Per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, Sentry.logger logs, and db spans through the esbuild orchestrion build.
  • Parameterized route names (GET /users/:id, source route) in both trace lifecycles, with the main.ts middleware above.

Known limits:

  • Nothing a route handler, middleware or page component throws reaches Sentry on its own: Fresh's default error handler catches it and returns a 500, leaving only the request span marked internal_error. Adding Sentry.captureConsoleIntegration({ levels: ["error"] }) to the withSentry options turns Fresh's own console.error(error) into an event, at the cost of reporting every other console.error.
  • Out of the box there are no route names: the request span is named from the URL (static) or the HTTP method alone (stream). Fresh does call getActiveSpan()?.updateName() and set http.route through @opentelemetry/api, but @sentry/cloudflare registers no OpenTelemetry global, so those calls land on a no-op tracer.
  • sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() does not belong in Fresh's vite.config.ts: there is no Cloudflare worker environment for it to instrument, and adding it there is worse than a no-op, it injects the channels and then breaks the driver under workerd through Vite's CJS interop (createRequire(import.meta.url) with unresolvable module ids).
  • mysql 2.x is the driver this ran on; mysql2 is instrumented but its query fails on workerd unless you pass disableEval: true, which this cell did not exercise.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/fresh.mdx with the wiring above and link it from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23.
  • Add the missing-worker-environment caveat to guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:13-18, stating that in a Vite build with no Cloudflare worker environment the plugin breaks bundled drivers rather than doing nothing.

Add a NestJS on Cloudflare Workers page using the esbuild orchestrion plugin

Description

Nothing documents NestJS on Workers. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/nestjs/index.mdx:5-9 sets fallbackGuide: javascript.node with server-node categories and carries no runtime caveat. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/ has no nestjs.mdx, the framework list at guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 omits Nest, httpServerHandler appears nowhere under docs/ or platform-includes/, and sentryOrchestrionPlugin appears exactly once, in the v10-to-v11 migration guide at common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:1297, with no how-to page behind it.

Verified on the develop line: NestJS 11 with @nestjs/platform-express on workerd, compatibility_date: "2026-05-01", compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat", "enable_nodejs_http_modules", "enable_nodejs_http_server_modules"], served through httpServerHandler from cloudflare:node and wrapped with Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare. Nest must be booted lazily inside fetch, not at module scope. Bundle the Worker with esbuild plus sentryOrchestrionPlugin() from @sentry/server-utils/orchestrion/esbuild and point wrangler's main at the output. Three esbuild details decide whether the bundle boots at all: a banner whose createRequire shim strips the node: prefix (workerd's createRequire cannot resolve node:-prefixed builtins, and node:process additionally needs an alias returning globalThis.process), aliases stubbing class-validator, class-transformer, @nestjs/microservices* and @nestjs/websockets/socket-module, and leaving mainFields/conditions alone, because preferring ESM hands Express the ESM build of is-promise and the Worker dies on the first request. Error capture uses exactly the Node wiring: install @sentry/nestjs next to @sentry/cloudflare and register SentryModule.forRoot() in imports and SentryGlobalFilter from @sentry/nestjs/setup as an APP_FILTER provider.

Works today:

  • Unhandled controller errors through SentryGlobalFilter (mechanism auto.http.nestjs.global_filter, handled: false), parameterized route names with middleware and handler spans, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, db spans, Sentry.logger logs.
  • Expected HttpExceptions stay unreported (a NotFoundException yields status not_found and no event), the same behavior as on Node.
  • Error capture needs no bundler plugin: SentryGlobalFilter works on a plain wrangler build too, since @sentry/nestjs/setup depends only on @nestjs/* and @sentry/core.

Known limits:

  • Plain wrangler bundling gives no route names and no database spans: those need the orchestrion esbuild build.
  • mysql2 on workerd needs disableEval: true in the connection options, and the connection must be opened inside the request handler rather than at module scope.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/nestjs.mdx with the wiring above and link it from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23.
  • Put the disableEval and connection-placement rules in a shared Workers database-driver note.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F43, cell M-nestjs-cf

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/nestjs.mdx (new), linked from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23; the mysql2 disableEval requirement belongs in a shared Workers database-driver note.

A page must say that NestJS 11 with the Express platform runs on workerd behind the node:http bridge, and that route names, handler spans and database spans need a hand-built esbuild bundle plus the orchestrion esbuild plugin; plain wrangler bundling is not sufficient for those. It must spell that build out, because the bundle does not boot without it: a createRequire banner that strips the node: prefix, stub aliases for class-validator, class-transformer, @nestjs/microservices* and @nestjs/websockets/socket-module, and no ESM-preferring mainFields or conditions, which would break express's require('is-promise'). Error capture is the exception, it needs no plugin and uses exactly the Node wiring: install @sentry/nestjs next to @sentry/cloudflare and register SentryModule.forRoot and the SentryGlobalFilter as APP_FILTER, with nothing Cloudflare-specific; expected HttpExceptions stay unreported, as on Node. Database spans require the build-time transform, work with both mysql drivers, and mysql2 additionally needs disableEval true or workerd rejects its generated row parsers; the connection must be opened inside the request, not at module scope. Isolation, incoming trace, outgoing fetch and logs work as-is on either build; route names, middleware and handler spans arrive with the orchestrion build.

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/sveltekit.mdx — replace the bare include passthrough with a page that keeps the include and adds "Route Names on Cloudflare Workers" (the raw-URL naming plus the intermediate `handle` workaround) and "Database Spans" (the npm/Yarn `ssr.noExternal` three-package requirement) sections

Two things the run needed are absent from the include. (1) Route names are confirmed broken on Workers: unlike the Node SvelteKit SDK, the transaction/segment span is named by the Cloudflare request handler and keeps the raw URL — `GET /users/42` with `transaction_info.source: url` under `traceLifecycle: 'static'`, and bare `GET` with source `url` under the default `traceLifecycle: 'stream'` — never the SvelteKit route id. The break is not limited to parameterized routes: every route except `/` loses its name, because the Cloudflare wrapper keeps only a name whose source is already `route`, and SvelteKit's own `kit.experimental.tracing.server` makes it worse by removing the route-named http.server span altogether. The page must state this and show the verified fix: a user-land `handle` inserted between `initCloudflareSentryHandle` and `sentryHandle` in the `sequence`, which takes `getRootSpan(getActiveSpan())` from `@sentry/sveltekit`, calls `updateName` with the method and `event.route.id` and sets `sentry.segment.name.source` to `route` plus `http.route` — not `Sentry.updateSpanName`, which stamps the source `custom`; it works in both lifecycles. (2) Database spans come from the build-time transform that `sentrySvelteKit()` (:369) already installs, so no extra setup is needed and the connection may live at module scope or inside the handler — but only on pnpm's isolated `node_modules`. On a flat `node_modules` (npm or Yarn) `vite build` fails in SvelteKit's analyse step with "The requested module '@sentry/core' does not provide an export named 'default'" until `ssr: { noExternal: ['@sentry/core', '@sentry/conventions', '@opentelemetry/api'] }` is added to `vite.config.js`. Do not widen that to a `/^@sentry\//` regex: it force-bundles `@sentry/sveltekit` and boots the Node SDK inside the Worker instead of the Cloudflare one.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/sveltekit.mdx:1-9 ("SvelteKit on Cloudflare", renders the shared include). The Cloudflare branch is platform-includes/getting-started-complete/javascript.sveltekit.mdx:299-347 — :310-343 the `sequence(initCloudflareSentryHandle({...}), sentryHandle())` snippet, :340-342 `handleErrorWithSentry`; plus :351-376 "Configure Vite" with `sentrySvelteKit()` before `sveltekit()` at :369, and :378-387 "Configure Cloudflare for Sentry" (wrangler via getting-started-config/javascript.cloudflare.mdx:5-21). :44 points adapter-cloudflare users at the Cloudflare guide; docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:22 links back. Missing: `grep -rn noExternal docs/platforms/javascript platform-includes` returns zero hits, and `grep -rn -i "transaction_info|route name|segment name" docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/` returns zero hits.

What ran: SvelteKit 2.70.3 (svelte 5.57.0, @sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare 5.1.0, vite 6.4.3) with @sentry/sveltekit 10.67.0 (develop, built from worktree audit-runtime-docs) on Cloudflare Workers (wrangler 4.129.0 dev, workerd 1.20260903.1, compatibility_date 2026-05-01, nodejs_compat). Cell record: M-sveltekit-cf.

DOC-F50Remix on Cloudflare: documented wrongP0proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Rewrite the Remix on Cloudflare page for Workers and fix the Vite plugin options

Description

docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx is 60 lines and does not describe a working Workers setup: :39-49 installs only @sentry/remix with no @sentry/cloudflare, and :55-58 embeds the shared include. In that include, the Cloudflare branch at platform-includes/getting-started-complete/javascript.remix.mdx:250-294 covers Cloudflare Pages only, and the "Capture Server-Side Errors" step at :296-329 sits outside any PlatformSection and tells every reader to import from bare @sentry/remix at :314. Separately, guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30 presents _experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection as something you must enable.

Verified on the develop line: Remix v2 (2.17.5) with the Remix Vite plugin on workerd, compatibility_date: "2026-05-01", compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat"]. Install @sentry/remix and @sentry/cloudflare; npm needs --legacy-peer-deps, because @remix-run/cloudflare pins @cloudflare/workers-types ^4 while wrangler 4 pins ^5. In app/entry.server.tsx, export const handleError = sentryHandleError imported from @sentry/remix/cloudflare. In the worker entry, Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare around a handler built with createRequestHandler(instrumentBuild(remixBuild)); do not reach for makeWrappedCreateRequestHandler from @sentry/remix/cloudflare as well, which on Cloudflare reports every request twice, as two http.server transactions in two different traces. In vite.config.ts, sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() from @sentry/cloudflare/vite at its defaults alongside the Remix plugin, with ssr: { target: 'webworker', noExternal: ['mysql', /^@sentry\//, '@opentelemetry/api'] } (noExternal: true is the blunt version of the same thing). Build with remix vite:build: a plain vite build under Vite 6 silently builds only the client and skips build/server.

This item prescribes sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() because that is what was run. The Remix Vite plugin's own Cloudflare behavior, sentryRemixVitePlugin from @sentry/remix/vite, which on the v11 line applies the build-time instrumentation transform itself, was not exercised in this audit.

Works today:

  • Loader, action and resource-route errors, parameterized route names with http.route, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, db spans from mysql, Sentry.logger logs.
  • Per-request isolation, with the withSentry plus instrumentBuild entry above.

Known limits:

  • Importing from bare @sentry/remix in a webworker SSR build leaves handleError undefined and captures nothing.
  • wrapRequestHandler from @sentry/cloudflare/request establishes no request isolation on Workers: it is the one Cloudflare wrapper that never calls setAsyncLocalStorageAsyncContextStrategy, so under concurrency every setTag, setUser and request-data write is global to the isolate and bleeds into unrelated later requests, and no event carries request data at all. It exists for runtimes that cannot enable nodejs_compat, such as Shopify Oxygen.
  • A route with no loader or action stays method-only.
  • With instrumentBuild alone the event's transaction field is unset, because nothing calls isolationScope.setTransactionName, so error grouping loses the route.
  • Without sentryCloudflareVitePlugin() there are no database spans and no warning; mysql2 is unusable on workerd.

What to change:

  • Rewrite guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx: add @sentry/cloudflare to the install at :39-49, add a Workers entry section, point handleError at @sentry/remix/cloudflare, and add a database-spans section.
  • Correct guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx to the shipped top-level options buildTimeInstrumentation and autoInstrumentation, both defaulting to true: the prose at :30, the snippets at :40-47 and the SdkOption blocks at :150-166, which still document _experimental.autoInstrumentation and _experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection as defaulting to false.
  • Add the wrapRequestHandler isolation warning to the two Hydrogen pages that document exactly that shape, guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hydrogen-remix.mdx and guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hydrogen-react-router.mdx.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F50, cell M-remix-cf

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx — rewrite to cover Workers: add `@sentry/cloudflare` to the install at :39-49, add a Workers entry section (`withSentry` + `instrumentBuild`), override the include's `@sentry/remix` import with `@sentry/remix/cloudflare`, and add a database-spans section (`sentryCloudflareVitePlugin()` plus `ssr.noExternal`); additionally correct docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30,40-47,150-166 where `useDiagnosticsChannelInjection` is presented as required, and add the `wrapRequestHandler` isolation warning to the two Hydrogen pages

The page contradicts the run on three points and must be rewritten for Workers. (1) Error capture: on Cloudflare Workers and Pages the `handleError` import must come from `@sentry/remix/cloudflare`, never bare `@sentry/remix` — the bare specifier silently captures nothing in a `webworker` SSR build. Both `export const handleError = sentryHandleError` and `wrapHandleErrorWithSentry` work from that subpath, on both trace lifecycles, for loaders, actions, resource routes and thrown 5xx Responses. (2) Request isolation: the worker entry must be wrapped with `withSentry` from `@sentry/cloudflare` around a handler built with `instrumentBuild` from `@sentry/remix/cloudflare`, and `makeWrappedCreateRequestHandler` must not be added on top, because on Cloudflare it double-reports every request as two http.server transactions in two different traces. `wrapRequestHandler` from `@sentry/cloudflare/request` exists only for runtimes that cannot enable `nodejs_compat` (Shopify Oxygen); on Workers it is the one wrapper that never calls `setAsyncLocalStorageAsyncContextStrategy`, so tags, user and request data are written to a scope shared by the whole isolate and bleed across concurrent requests, and events carry no request data — a warning the two Hydrogen pages, which document exactly that shape, should carry. (3) Database spans require the build-time transform: add `sentryCloudflareVitePlugin()` to the Vite config alongside the Remix plugin — defaults are sufficient, `autoInstrumentation: false` is unnecessary, and the connection may sit at module scope or inside the loader — and set `ssr: { target: 'webworker', noExternal: ['mysql', /^@sentry\//, '@opentelemetry/api'] }`, with `noExternal: true` as the blunt equivalent. Without the plugin, queries run normally, no `db` span appears, and the SDK logs no warning. Two build details belong on the page as well: the build command is `remix vite:build`, since a plain `vite build` under Vite 6 skips the server build, and npm needs `--legacy-peer-deps` for the `@cloudflare/workers-types` conflict between `@remix-run/cloudflare` and wrangler 4. The `_experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection: true` requirement stated on the Vite plugin page is no longer needed on the v11 line and must be corrected there too. Finally, the page must also install `@sentry/cloudflare`, not just `@sentry/remix`. One scope note: this item prescribes `sentryCloudflareVitePlugin()` because that is the plugin the run used; `sentryRemixVitePlugin` from `@sentry/remix/vite`, which on the v11 line applies the build-time transform itself, was not exercised on Cloudflare in this audit and may well be the shorter path.

Where the docs stand: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/remix.mdx is 60 lines and is wrong for Workers: :39-49 installs only `@sentry/remix` (no `@sentry/cloudflare`), and :55-58 embeds the shared include. In that include, the Cloudflare branch platform-includes/getting-started-complete/javascript.remix.mdx:250-294 covers Cloudflare Pages only — a `functions/_middleware.js` using `Sentry.sentryPagesPlugin` (:261-288) — with no Workers entry at all. The "Capture Server-Side Errors" step at :296-329 is outside any PlatformSection and tells every reader, Cloudflare included, to `import * as Sentry from "@sentry/remix"` (:314) and assign `Sentry.wrapHandleErrorWithSentry` (:316) / `Sentry.sentryHandleError` (:323). The run contradicts this: the bare specifier resolves to the browser build in the Remix Vite `webworker` SSR build, leaving `handleError` undefined and capturing nothing, with only a rollup warning. `instrumentBuild` and `@sentry/remix/cloudflare` appear in the docs only on the Oxygen page, docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/hydrogen-remix.mdx:32, :42 and :91. `grep -rn noExternal` over docs and platform-includes returns zero hits. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx:30 tells users to enable `_experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection`, and :150-166 documents it and `_experimental.autoInstrumentation` as defaulting to false, against packages/cloudflare/src/vite/index.ts:37 and :52-54 where `buildTimeInstrumentation` and `autoInstrumentation` are top-level and both default true.

What ran: Remix v2 (2.17.5, Remix Vite plugin, resource routes) on Cloudflare Workers (wrangler 4.129.0 dev / workerd, compatibility_date 2026-05-01, nodejs_compat). Cell record: M-remix-cf.

DOC-F51SolidStart on Cloudflare: documented noP1proven

Proposed issue new-page

Add a SolidStart on Cloudflare Workers page wiring the Nitro output with withSentry

Description

Nothing documents this combination in either direction. grep -rn -i cloudflare docs/platforms/javascript/guides/solidstart/ returns zero hits across index.mdx, install/, features/ and profiling/. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/ has no solidstart.mdx and the framework list at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 omits SolidStart. The generic Workers entry pattern the run relied on is documented only at guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:111, with no SolidStart context.

Verified on the develop line: SolidStart 1.3 with the cloudflare-module Nitro preset, compatibility_date: "2026-05-01", compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat"]. @sentry/solidstart itself does not run on Workers, so keep it for the browser and wire the server side by hand: set server: { preset: 'cloudflare-module', rollupConfig: { external: ['@sentry/cloudflare'] } } in app.config.ts so Nitro does not inline a second SDK copy, then write a worker.mjs that wraps the built Nitro handler with Sentry.withSentry() from @sentry/cloudflare and point wrangler's main at that wrapper. Add a Nitro server plugin registered through server.plugins that forwards Nitro's error hook to Sentry.captureException, otherwise thrown server errors never become events. Nitro emits no wrangler config for the cloudflare-module preset, so write wrangler.jsonc by hand: main pointing at the wrapper, plus an assets entry for .output/public with its ASSETS binding. Do not skip rollupConfig.external: without it Nitro inlines a second @sentry/cloudflare, and the failure is silent rather than loud, because Sentry.setTag and Sentry.captureException in a route handler then run against an uninitialised client while the wrapper's request transactions keep arriving and make the setup look healthy.

Works today:

  • Server errors through the Nitro error hook, per-request isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch spans with propagation headers, Sentry.logger logs, and browser errors with Sentry.init from @sentry/solidstart in src/entry-client.tsx.

Known limits:

  • Server transactions never carry the file-route pattern: under traceLifecycle: 'static' the name is the raw request URL with source url, and under the default 'stream' the path is gone altogether and the span is named bare GET. There is no Sentry SolidStart router integration on this runtime.
  • Errors thrown while a page renders on the server stay uncaptured even with the Nitro error plugin, because SolidStart streams the render through a detached pipe.
  • There is no server-to-client trace continuation: nothing injects a sentry-trace meta tag into the SSR HTML.
  • Database spans were not exercised, and no Sentry database instrumentation path exists for this vinxi/Nitro/rollup build shape.

What to change:

  • Add docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/solidstart.mdx with the wiring above and the limits.
  • Show the route-name fix on that page: src/middleware.ts, registered with middleware: './src/middleware.ts' in app.config.ts, that builds a radix3 router from the vinxi/routes file-route table, looks the request path up in it, and on Sentry.getRootSpan(Sentry.getActiveSpan()) from @sentry/cloudflare calls updateName with the matched pattern and sets the segment name source to route.
  • Link it from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 and from guides/solidstart/index.mdx.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-F51, cell M-solidstart-cf

Page: docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/solidstart.mdx (new), linked from docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 and from docs/platforms/javascript/guides/solidstart/index.mdx

A page must exist and must open by stating that `@sentry/solidstart` itself does not run on Workers — its server entry initializes the Node SDK, and there is no `@sentry/solidstart/cloudflare` export or Cloudflare code path — so the runtime is wired by hand: keep `@sentry/solidstart` for the browser and wrap the built Nitro handler with `Sentry.withSentry` from `@sentry/cloudflare` in your own `worker.mjs`, keeping `@sentry/cloudflare` external via `server.rollupConfig` so Nitro does not inline a second copy. It must then document the two capabilities that need extra work beyond that wrapper. (1) Server errors are not captured by the wrapper alone: add a Nitro server plugin that forwards Nitro's `error` hook to `Sentry.captureException` and register it with `server.plugins: ['./src/server-plugin.ts']` in `app.config.ts`. (2) Route names are wrong in both trace lifecycles — the raw URL with source `url` under `static`, bare `GET` under the default `stream` — so add `src/middleware.ts` (registered via `middleware: './src/middleware.ts'` in `app.config.ts`) that builds a radix3 router from vinxi's route manifest and renames the Cloudflare root span per request using `getActiveSpan`/`getRootSpan`/`spanToJSON` and the segment-name-source attribute. Two more things the page has to say: wrangler.jsonc must be written by hand, since Nitro emits none for the `cloudflare-module` preset, and omitting `server.rollupConfig.external` fails silently, because the inlined second copy of `@sentry/cloudflare` leaves handler-side Sentry calls as no-ops while request transactions keep arriving. With all of that in place isolation, incoming trace continuation, outgoing fetch, logs and client-side errors all work, but errors thrown during server-side page rendering stay uncaptured; database spans were not exercised.

Where the docs stand: No page mentions this combination in either direction. `grep -rn -i cloudflare docs/platforms/javascript/guides/solidstart/` returns zero hits across index.mdx, install/, features/ and profiling/. `ls docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/frameworks/` contains astro, hono, hydrogen-react-router, hydrogen-remix, nextjs, nuxt, remix, sveltekit and tanstack-start — there is no solidstart.mdx — and the framework list at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:16-23 omits SolidStart. SolidStart appears in the docs only inside PlatformSection exclusion lists, for example docs/platforms/javascript/common/tracing/streamed-spans/index.mdx:76 and docs/platforms/javascript/common/index.mdx:31. The generic Cloudflare `withSentry` entry pattern the run relied on is documented only at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:111 ("Setup for Cloudflare Workers"), with no SolidStart context.

What ran: SolidStart 1.3 (vinxi 0.5 + Nitro, `cloudflare-module` preset) on Cloudflare Workers (workerd via wrangler 4.129.0 dev, compatibility_date 2026-05-01, nodejs_compat). Cell record: M-solidstart-cf.

6. Found by the completeness check

A final pass on 2026-09-07 compared the probe list with the SDK exports on develop and with every docs page shown to these runtimes. It surfaced docs defects that the earlier items had not covered, plus a list of things this audit did not measure. The defects below were each re-verified against the docs file and the SDK source before being written up; each carries a proposed issue like the items above.

DOC-R1Stream-mode includes call `'static'` the default; develop defaults to `'stream'`P0verified in source

Proposed issue docs-change

Fix inverted traceLifecycle default in the stream mode includes

Description

The three stream-mode includes present stream mode as opt-in, in four places. platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.bun.mdx:5, javascript.deno.mdx:5 and javascript.cloudflare.mdx:8 (Cloudflare Pages) and :35 (Cloudflare Workers) all say "Opt in by setting the traceLifecycle option to 'stream'", and each file closes with "set traceLifecycle to 'static' (the default)" (javascript.bun.mdx:25, javascript.deno.mdx:24, javascript.cloudflare.mdx:62).

On develop the default is 'stream'. packages/core/src/client.ts:243 (:242 at the pin) normalizes traceLifecycle: options.traceLifecycle === 'static' ? 'static' : 'stream', and the comment directly above it (:237-238) states the same rule: every value except an explicit 'static' resolves to stream. No runtime overrides it. Cloudflare reads SENTRY_TRACE_LIFECYCLE at packages/cloudflare/src/options.ts:73, but that read returns undefined when the variable is unset (:107-109), so it never changes the default, and packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-serve.ts:28 describes 'stream' as the default too.

The docs already say this on another page: docs/platforms/javascript/common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:23 calls stream mode "the new default", and :198 tells the reader to set traceLifecycle: 'static', or the SENTRY_TRACE_LIFECYCLE=static environment variable, to keep the previous model. The includes contradict the migration guide on the same site.

What to change

  • Reframe all four opt-in sentences as opt-out (javascript.bun.mdx:5, javascript.deno.mdx:5, javascript.cloudflare.mdx:8 and :35): 'stream' is the default, 'static' is what a user sets.
  • Replace the traceLifecycle: "stream" sample lines with "static" examples, or drop them.
  • Add the missing comma after tracesSampleRate: 0.2 in the Pages snippet at javascript.cloudflare.mdx:19; the Workers snippet at :40-56 already parses.
  • Match the wording of migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:23 and :198, including the SENTRY_TRACE_LIFECYCLE=static escape hatch.

Confirmed on Node on 2026-09-09: with no traceLifecycle option every Elysia span carried sentry.trace_lifecycle: stream and only span envelopes arrived; transaction envelopes needed an explicit 'static' (QS-elysia, variants guide and static).

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R1

Status quo. platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.bun.mdx:5, javascript.deno.mdx:5 and javascript.cloudflare.mdx:8 (Pages) and :35 (Workers) all open with "Opt in by setting the `traceLifecycle` option to `'stream'`". Each file closes with "To revert to transaction mode, set `traceLifecycle` to `'static'` (the default)" (bun:25, deno:24, cloudflare:62).

On develop. packages/core/src/client.ts:243 on develop (:242 at the pin) normalizes `traceLifecycle: options.traceLifecycle === 'static' ? 'static' : 'stream'`, and the comment above it at :237-238 states the same rule, so every value other than an explicit `'static'` resolves to stream. No runtime overrides it: the Cloudflare env read at packages/cloudflare/src/options.ts:73 returns undefined when `SENTRY_TRACE_LIFECYCLE` is unset (:107-109), and packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-serve.ts:28 also calls `'stream'` the default. docs/platforms/javascript/common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:23 and :198 already document stream as the new default, so master contradicts itself.

Work item. Reframe all four opt-in sentences as opt-out: stream is the default and `'static'` is the option a user sets. Also fix the missing comma after `tracesSampleRate: 0.2` in the Pages snippet at javascript.cloudflare.mdx:19, which makes that snippet a SyntaxError; the Workers snippet at :40-56 is already valid.

DOC-R2Deno crons page recommends denoCronIntegration(), which throws on Deno 2.9.0 and laterP0verified in source

Proposed issue docs-change

Stop recommending denoCronIntegration() on the Deno crons page

Description

platform-includes/crons/setup/javascript.deno.mdx:1 heads its first section "Automatic Check-Ins (Recommended)" and :12 tells the reader to pass Sentry.denoCronIntegration() to Sentry.init.

That call crashes startup on Deno 2.9.0 and later. packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-cron.ts:23 assigns Deno.cron = new Proxy(Deno.cron, ...) inside setupOnce, i.e. inside Sentry.init. On Deno 2.8.3 Deno.cron is a writable data property and the integration works: a cron firing at the minute boundary produced one in_progress and one ok check-in. From Deno 2.9.0 Deno.cron is a getter-only accessor, so the assignment throws TypeError: Cannot set property cron of #<Object> which has only a getter and the process exits 1 before any user code runs.

There is a second failure mode without the unstable flag. When Deno.cron is absent the guard at deno-cron.ts:18-21 returns silently, so the integration is an inert no-op and the SDK gives no warning, while the reader's own Deno.cron(...) call throws TypeError: Deno.cron is not a function. The integration is also not in the Deno default set (packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:31-49), although its own JSDoc at deno-cron.ts:64 says "Enabled by default in the Deno SDK".

What to change

  • Remove the "Recommended" framing and the denoCronIntegration() snippet, or state that it makes Sentry.init throw on Deno 2.9.0 and later and works only up to Deno 2.8.3.
  • Lead with the Sentry.withMonitor / Sentry.captureCheckIn sections already on the page.
  • State that Deno.cron needs --unstable-cron, and that without the flag the integration silently does nothing.
  • Say that the integration has to be passed in integrations explicitly; it is not in the Deno default set.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R2

Status quo. platform-includes/crons/setup/javascript.deno.mdx:1 heads the section "Automatic Check-Ins (Recommended)" and :12 shows `integrations: [Sentry.denoCronIntegration()]` as the way to monitor `Deno.cron` calls.

On develop. packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-cron.ts:23 assigns `Deno.cron = new Proxy(Deno.cron, ...)` in `setupOnce`. probes/D11 recorded the version split: on Deno 2.8.3 `Deno.cron` is a writable data property and the integration works (one `in_progress` and one `ok` check-in for monitor slug `probe`); on Deno 2.9.0 it is a getter-only accessor, so `Sentry.init` throws `TypeError: Cannot set property cron of #<Object> which has only a getter` and the process exits 1. Without `--unstable-cron` the guard at deno-cron.ts:18-21 returns silently, so the integration is an inert no-op and the user's own `Deno.cron(...)` throws `TypeError: Deno.cron is not a function`. The integration is also absent from the default set at packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:31-49, contradicting its own JSDoc at deno-cron.ts:64.

Work item. Drop the "Recommended" framing and the `denoCronIntegration()` snippet, or mark the integration as broken on Deno 2.9.0 and later. Lead the page with the `Sentry.withMonitor` / `Sentry.captureCheckIn` path that the other sections already document, state the `--unstable-cron` requirement and the silent no-op without it, and say the integration must be added explicitly. SDK side, D11 also recorded that `Object.defineProperty(Deno, 'cron', { value, writable: true, configurable: true })` succeeds on both Deno versions, so a one-line SDK fix would restore the integration; the docs change should link that follow-up rather than only steering readers away, and the JSDoc-versus-defaults contradiction is a separate SDK bug.

DOC-R7OpenTelemetry pages gated to Bun; Deno sets up a tracer by default and Cloudflare opts inP2verified in source

Proposed issue docs-change

Add Deno and Cloudflare to the OpenTelemetry overview and API pages

Description

The three OpenTelemetry pages gate themselves to a supported allowlist that includes javascript.bun but neither javascript.deno nor javascript.cloudflare: docs/platforms/javascript/common/opentelemetry/index.mdx:11, custom-setup.mdx:9 and using-opentelemetry-apis.mdx:9. Deno and Cloudflare readers never see the section.

Both runtimes support the setup on develop, with different defaults. packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:114 calls setupOpenTelemetryTracer() when options.enableOpenTelemetrySetup ?? true, so a Deno app gets a Sentry OpenTelemetry tracer provider unless it passes enableOpenTelemetrySetup: false, and spans created through @opentelemetry/api reach Sentry with no extra configuration. packages/cloudflare/src/sdk.ts:23 sets options.enableOpenTelemetrySetup ??= false and installs the tracer at :27-29 only when the option is true. Bun, already on the list, is off by default too: it delegates to @sentry/node, where packages/node/src/sdk/index.ts:253 applies options.enableOpenTelemetrySetup ?? false.

custom-setup.mdx is the exception and should stay gated for now. Its body is written around skipOpenTelemetrySetup (:35, :122, :132), SentryContextManager (:35, :53), SentrySpanProcessor (:53, :66) and SentrySampler (:53), none of which exist on develop, and the include it pulls for JavaScript readers is written against the same removed API. There is no javascript.deno.mdx or javascript.cloudflare.mdx under platform-includes/performance/opentelemetry-setup/, so the two guides would fall back to javascript.mdx and get a page that cannot be followed.

What to change

  • Add - javascript.deno and - javascript.cloudflare to the supported lists in opentelemetry/index.mdx and using-opentelemetry-apis.mdx, after checking those two bodies for Node-only wording.
  • State the differing defaults: Deno sets up the tracer unless enableOpenTelemetrySetup: false, Cloudflare only with enableOpenTelemetrySetup: true, Bun off unless it is turned on.
  • Leave custom-setup.mdx:9 as it is until that page and the performance/opentelemetry-setup includes are dealt with.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R7

Status quo. docs/platforms/javascript/common/opentelemetry/index.mdx:11, custom-setup.mdx:9 and using-opentelemetry-apis.mdx:9 list `javascript.bun` in their `supported` allowlists. Neither `javascript.deno` nor `javascript.cloudflare` appears, so the whole OpenTelemetry section is hidden from those two guides.

On develop. packages/deno/src/sdk.ts:114 runs `setupOpenTelemetryTracer()` whenever `options.enableOpenTelemetrySetup ?? true`, so Deno installs a Sentry OpenTelemetry tracer provider unless the option is set to `false`. packages/cloudflare/src/sdk.ts:23 sets the same option to `false` by default and installs the tracer at :27-29 only when it is `true`. What to change

  • Rewrite the three snippets as a runnable call with concrete values, for example a real root path and prefix string.
  • Move the per-option descriptions out of the object literal into an Options section.
  • Put the Cloudflare example in a withSentry or sentryPagesPlugin options object instead of Sentry.init, and keep the ___SDK_PACKAGE___ import placeholder in the Deno snippet.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R11

Status quo. platform-includes/configuration/rewrite-frames/javascript.bun.mdx:6-17, javascript.deno.mdx:6-17 and javascript.cloudflare.mdx:6-17 pass `rewriteFramesIntegration` an object literal whose members are type annotations: `root: string;`, `prefix: string;` and `iteratee: (frame) => frame;`, inside a fence tagged ```javascript with tabTitle "JavaScript".

On develop. Pasted as written this is a SyntaxError in every runtime: `string` is an undefined identifier used as a value, and the trailing semicolons are invalid inside an object literal. The integration itself is fine and exported by all three SDKs (packages/bun/src/index.ts:196 on develop and :194 at the pin, packages/deno/src/index.ts:79 and packages/cloudflare/src/index.ts:82, both unchanged); only the snippet is broken. The Cloudflare file also calls a bare `Sentry.init` at :4, while that SDK is configured through `withSentry` or `sentryPagesPlugin`.

Work item. Replace the type-annotation block with a runnable call using concrete values, and move the option descriptions into an Options section or into comments alongside real arguments.   • Say that the entry still needs import "@sentry/deno/import" for library spans.

Status quo. The Deno guide's only run commands are `deno run --allow-net=___ORG_INGEST_DOMAIN___ index.ts` (docs/platforms/javascript/guides/deno/index.mdx:100) and `deno run --allow-read=./src index.ts` (:119). The string `deno serve` appears nowhere under docs/, platform-includes/ or includes/.

On develop. An `export default { fetch }` entrypoint started with `deno serve` is instrumented exactly like `Deno.serve`, because the DenoServe integration proxies `Deno.serve` (packages/deno/src/integrations/deno-serve.ts:80-93 on develop, :75-88 at the pin, the shift coming from a new `@deprecated` JSDoc on `ignoreStatusCodes`), which `deno serve` calls internally. probes/N8 recorded what `--parallel` does: 16 workers in one process, each with its own `Sentry.init` and its own module-scope load, 55 requests producing exactly 55 spans, per-request tags matching their worker in 10 of 10 events, and no session envelope at all, since the Deno SDK ships no release health. The same probe recorded that a docs-shaped entry without `import "@sentry/deno/import"` still reports errors and `http.server` spans under `deno serve` but loses library spans, the mysql `db` span included.

Work item. Add a `deno serve` note to the Deno guide stating that a default-export `fetch` handler is instrumented the same way, that `--parallel` initializes once per worker without duplicating events or leaking scope data between workers, and that the `@sentry/deno/import` hook line is still what buys library spans.

DOC-R19Cloudflare Pages middleware snippet does not say the root middleware also spans static asset requestsP2verified in source

Proposed issue docs-change

Note that the root Pages middleware also spans static asset requests

Description

The Cloudflare Pages setup at docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:161-205 tells the reader to register sentryPagesPlugin as the first entry in onRequest inside functions/_middleware.js (snippet at :174). includes/cloudflare-pages-middleware-intro.mdx instructs creating that file in the functions directory at the project root. Neither says which requests the middleware then handles.

A root functions/_middleware.js runs on every Cloudflare Pages request, static asset requests included. Under the default stream lifecycle each asset fetch produces its own http.server span envelope alongside the app's function routes, which changes both the trace volume and what the reader sees in the performance view; the same requests become transactions only with traceLifecycle: "static". The naming is worth stating too: the root request span is named GET / and every other path is named bare GET, with the path carried in the url.path attribute.

What to change

  • Add a note to the Pages setup section stating that a root middleware runs on every request, static assets included.
  • Say what arrives per request under the default stream lifecycle: one http.server span envelope, named GET / for the root and GET for every other path, with url.path as an attribute.
  • Point readers at scoping the middleware to a subdirectory, for example functions/api/_middleware.js, when they want only their function routes instrumented.

Confirmed on 2026-09-09 with wrangler 4.130.0: the root functions/_middleware.js produced an http.server span envelope for GET / and for GET /style.css, requests with no function at all; moved to functions/api/_middleware.js it instrumented /api/* only and silently dropped the guide's own root functions/debug-sentry.js verify route, so the scoped remedy needs the note that the verify route must then live under that directory (QS-cloudflare-pages, variants base and scoped).

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-R19

Status quo. docs/platforms/javascript/guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:161-205 tells the reader to add `sentryPagesPlugin` as the first middleware in `functions/_middleware.js` (snippet at :174), and includes/cloudflare-pages-middleware-intro.mdx instructs creating that file in the `functions` directory at the project root. Neither says what requests the middleware then runs on.

On develop. probes/C10 drove a Pages project with a root `functions/_middleware.js` under the default stream lifecycle: every request produced an `http.server` span envelope, the static assets `/` and `/app.js` included. Under stream mode those are span envelopes, not transactions; transactions appear only with `traceLifecycle: "static"`. C10 also recorded the naming: `GET /` for the root and bare `GET` for every other path, with `url.path` as an attribute.

Work item. Add a note to the Pages setup section stating that a root middleware runs on every request including static assets, that each one arrives as its own span envelope under the default stream lifecycle, and that scoping the middleware to a subdirectory such as `functions/api/_middleware.js` limits instrumentation to function routes.

DOC-Q1The verify step of all four guides is written around the transaction envelope that v11 stops sendingP1provenlands with v11

Proposed issue docs-change

Rewrite the tracing verify step for the stream lifecycle on the Node, Bun, Deno and Cloudflare guides

Description

The tracing verify step tells the reader to wrap work in Sentry.startSpan({ op: "test", name: "My First Test Transaction" }): platform-includes/getting-started-verify/javascript.node.mdx:38-53, guides/bun/index.mdx:156-172, guides/deno/index.mdx:176-190 and guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:313-335. The sentence that follows each one sends the reader to Sentry "to view the collected data" (getting-started-node/javascript.mdx:120, bun/index.mdx:188, deno/index.mdx:207, cloudflare/index.mdx:364) and expands the quick-start-locate-data-expandable include, which points at the Traces page. The only place the word "Transaction" appears is the sample name inside the snippet.

Observed on develop, on all four runtimes with the documented wiring: no transaction envelope is sent at all. The manual span arrives as a child item inside a span envelope together with the server span, for example ["http.client GET 127.0.0.1", "test My First Test Span", "http.server GET"], because packages/core/src/client.ts:243 normalizes any traceLifecycle other than 'static' to 'stream'. On the released line there is no such normalization, so 'static' is the default and a transaction is sent: the pages are correct for the version they serve today and become wrong with v11.

What to change, in the v11 docs update:

  • On each of the four verify steps, describe what arrives: a trace with a server segment span and the manual span as its child, no transaction.
  • Rename the sample from "My First Test Transaction" to a span name.
  • Link the stream lifecycle page, and note that traceLifecycle: "static" restores the transaction envelope for readers who look for it.
  • Fix the ten platform-includes/performance/enable-stream-mode/javascript.*.mdx files whose closing line calls traceLifecycle 'static' "(the default)": node :36, cloudflare :62, hono :51, deno :24, and bun, nextjs, nestjs, nitro, gcp-functions and elysia at :25.
  • Rewrite guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:382-388, which frames the static lifecycle as the current model and stream as the opt-in.
  • Apply the same rewrite to the framework verify includes: platform-includes/getting-started-verify/javascript.express.mdx:30 and :40 name "My First Test Transaction", and the Express run received the span as an op: test child of the http.server segment inside a span envelope, never a transaction envelope (QS-express, variants import and express5); getting-started-verify/javascript.fastify.mdx:24-30 and :40 and guides/fastify/tracing/instrumentation/automatic-instrumentation.mdx:3 say the same and the Fastify run saw the same (QS-fastify, every variant); getting-started-verify/javascript.koa.mdx:29 and :35-46 and guides/koa/tracing/instrumentation/automatic-instrumentation.mdx:3 likewise (QS-koa, every variant), as do getting-started-verify/javascript.hapi.mdx:34 and :47 and guides/hapi/tracing/instrumentation/automatic-instrumentation.mdx:3 (QS-hapi, every variant), and getting-started-verify/javascript.connect.mdx:30 and :36-47, guides/connect/tracing/instrumentation/automatic-instrumentation.mdx:3 and includes/node-automatic-instrumentation.mdx:1-3 (QS-connect, every variant that starts), and getting-started-verify/javascript.hono.mdx:30 plus the getting-started-config/javascript.hono.mdx:21 and :57 comment about 100% of transactions (QS-hono, every variant); the Hono guide page itself already says spans at guides/hono/index.mdx:184; the NestJS verify include is covered by DOC-Q57; the Google Cloud Functions config include says 100% of transactions at platform-includes/getting-started-config/javascript.gcp-functions.mdx:19 and its tracing/instrumentation/automatic-instrumentation.mdx:3 explains transactions, while the invocation arrives as a function.gcp segment with the test span as its child (QS-gcp-functions, variant http-tracing). The Pages run confirmed the Cloudflare paragraph at guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:382-384: with no traceLifecycle option every trace arrived as span items and the waitUntil spans as later span envelopes, so stream is the default and static the opt-out (QS-cloudflare-pages, variant base).

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-Q1, quick starts QS-node, QS-bun, QS-deno, QS-cloudflare

The four verify blocks and the four following sentences are exact on master. What the item first claimed about them is not: none of the four says the data appears as "named transactions". Each reads "head over to your project on Sentry.io ... to view the collected data" and expands an include whose tracing text is "Open the Traces page and select a trace to reveal more information about each span" (src/components/featureInfo.tsx:46-59). The mismatch is therefore between the snippet's own sample name and what the SDK sends, not a promise in the prose.

The item is also v11-scoped, like DOC-C1 to DOC-C5. On released master packages/core/src/client.ts does no traceLifecycle normalization, so 'static' is the default and a transaction envelope is sent; on develop, client.ts:243 normalizes anything but 'static' to 'stream'. The ten enable-stream-mode includes that call 'static' "the default" carry the same assumption and all become wrong at the same moment.

What ran: The four getting-started reproductions on 2026-09-08, develop 6f2da7483d: quickstarts/node, bun, deno, cloudflare, results file results-*.json in each. No variant produced a transaction envelope.

DOC-Q2The Cloudflare Vite plugin instruments nothing, without warning, when it resolves a different default-named wrangler configP1proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Say on the Vite plugin page that a wrangler config resolved to the wrong file instruments nothing

Description

guides/cloudflare/features/vite-plugin.mdx documents the probing order at :68 and gives wranglerConfigPath its own option entry at :130-148, described as the setting for a config at a custom path. What no page says is what happens when the config is not at a custom path but simply not the one the reader means: with several wrangler files in the project, the plugin resolves the first default-named wrangler.json, wrangler.jsonc or wrangler.toml at the Vite root, reads the entry point and classes from that file, and instruments nothing for the Worker actually being built. Nothing is logged, because the plugin only warns when no config parses at all.

Observed on develop with a Worker whose source has no withSentry call, built in a directory holding more than one wrangler config: before wranglerConfigPath was passed, the run produced an empty sink, an uncaught error in the worker log and no trace-propagation headers. With wranglerConfigPath set and an instrument.server.ts next to the wrangler main, the built Worker delivered the same errors, spans, headers and logs as a hand-written withSentry, with no experimental flag.

What to change:

  • On the Vite plugin page, state that wranglerConfigPath is required whenever the project holds more than one wrangler config, not only when the config sits at a custom path, and that a config resolved to the wrong file instruments nothing while the build still succeeds.
  • Say how to tell: with auto-instrumentation working, the built entry contains a withSentry wrapper; without it there is no wrapper and no plugin output.
  • Remove the _experimental.useDiagnosticsChannelInjection flag from guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:77-92; it is not needed on develop (also DOC-C4).

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-Q2, quick start QS-cloudflare, variant vite

Two of the three observations this item started with no longer hold. The instrument.server.* step is documented since commit 9bfe0e96c (sentry-docs#19199) at vite-plugin.mdx:88-99, with the defineCloudflareOptions snippet at :90-97 and, at :99, "If no instrument.server.* file exists, the SDK reads all configuration (DSN, release, environment, sample rate, etc.) from the Worker's env bindings at runtime." And the () => undefined fallback (ENV_FALLBACK_OPTIONS_FN, packages/cloudflare/src/vite/instrumentFile.ts:7) is not a silent failure but a deliberate hand-off: packages/cloudflare/src/options.ts:68 resolves dsn: userOptions.dsn ?? getEnvVar(env, 'SENTRY_DSN'), so the Worker runs without a DSN only when env carries none either. The plugin also does warn when no wrangler config parses, through the Vite logger (packages/cloudflare/src/vite/autoInstrument.ts:34-40): "[sentry] No parseable wrangler config found - auto-instrumentation disabled. Set wranglerConfigPath if your config uses a custom name."

What is left is the case that warning does not cover. The reproduction's own note (quickstarts/cloudflare/README.md:341-347) records it: the directory holds several wrangler configs, a different default-named one was found and used, and the plugin instrumented nothing - "items": [], an uncaught Error: My first Sentry error! in the worker log and {"sentry-trace":null,"baggage":null} at the recorder. A config was found, so no warning fired. Both plugins now get the file explicitly (cloudflare({ configPath }) and sentryCloudflareVitePlugin({ wranglerConfigPath })). The layout is a harness artifact; the failure mode it exposes is not.

What ran: Cloudflare getting-started reproduction, variant vite, on 2026-09-08 with wrangler 4.129.0 and @cloudflare/vite-plugin 1.54.4: quickstarts/cloudflare, results-vite.json. With wranglerConfigPath and the options file in place the variant matched the hand-written withSentry baseline on all ten integrations.

DOC-Q3Without a framework, server spans are named by method or URL on every runtime, and no guide says where a route name comes fromP1provenlands with v11

Proposed issue docs-change

Description

The four guides end their verify step without saying anything about how server spans are named: getting-started-node/javascript.mdx:120, guides/bun/index.mdx:188, guides/deno/index.mdx:207, guides/cloudflare/index.mdx:364 each send the reader to Sentry "to view the collected data" and expand an include that points at the Traces page. Nothing on the way there says where a route name comes from, so a reader who sees GET has no page to check.

Observed on develop with the base setup: a plain node:http server on Node names every segment span GET and sets no sentry.source; Deno.serve names every span GET with source url, a URLPattern route included; withSentry on Workers names every span GET with source url; only Bun.serve with a routes map produces GET /users/:id with source route, and falls back to GET for an unrouted path. On Bun and Cloudflare the error event carries no transaction field, so an issue shows no route either; on Node the same event does carry transaction: "GET /debug-sentry". Route names appear once a framework integration is in place, as the framework matrix records.

What to change, in the v11 docs update:

  • On each guide, state that the base setup names server spans by method (or URL under the static lifecycle) and that route names come from a framework integration, or on Bun from the Bun.serve routes map.
  • Link the framework pages for the runtime from that sentence.
  • On the Bun and Cloudflare guides, drop the claim that issues are grouped by transaction until the SDK sets the field. The Node guide can keep it: the Node error event does carry the transaction name.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-Q3, quick starts QS-node, QS-bun, QS-deno, QS-cloudflare

The four cited lines are exact, but the item's original framing was not: the pages do not promise named transactions. Each says "head over to your project on Sentry.io ... to view the collected data" and links the quick-start-locate-data-expandable include, which sends the reader to the Traces page. The word "transaction" is on none of them. The gap is an omission - nothing tells the reader what a span name depends on - not a false promise.

The reproduced naming holds as recorded: QS-node segment spans are GET with no sentry.source (quickstarts/node/README.md:114); QS-deno GET with source url; QS-cloudflare GET with source url; QS-bun GET /users/:id with source route from the Bun.serve routes map (quickstarts/bun/README.md:340) and GET for an unrouted path. The last bullet stays scoped to Bun and Cloudflare because Node differs: its error event carries transaction: "GET /debug-sentry" (quickstarts/node/README.md:235).

Like DOC-Q1, this is v11-scoped: under the released static lifecycle the reader does get a named transaction, so the guides are not wrong on the version they serve today.

What ran: The four getting-started reproductions on 2026-09-08: quickstarts/, the http.server items in every results-*.json.

DOC-Q4The documented Bun path logs two warnings about missing channel injection that no page explainsP2proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Explain the channel-injection warnings on the Bun guide

Description

Following the "Apply Instrumentation to Your App" step at guides/bun/index.mdx:82-105 as written, bun --preload ./instrument.ts app.ts (the command at :100) with debug: true prints No available Node API to register diagnostics-channel injection hooks; skipping. at startup and [Sentry] No diagnostics-channel injection detected. Channel-based integrations will not record spans. Make sure the diagnostics channels are injected via the runtime --import hook or a bundler plugin before the instrumented modules load. after the first request. Neither line is mentioned on the Bun guide or the troubleshooting pages, and the guide names no bundler plugin (DOC-B1).

Observed on develop with bun 1.4.2: the warnings are correct. The first comes from packages/server-runtime-injection/src/register.ts:160 (:201 at develop head), the second from packages/server-utils/src/orchestrion/detect.ts:38-42; both go through debug.warn, so they print only with debug: true. Runtime instrumentation (Bun.serve, fetch, errors, logs) works on the documented path, and library instrumentation needs the Bun.build with sentryBunPlugin(). A reader who sees the warning has no page that says which of the two it refers to.

What to change:

  • Quote both lines on the Bun guide next to the run command at :100, say that they print only with debug: true and that runtime instrumentation is unaffected, and link the plugin build step from DOC-B1 as the fix for library spans.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-Q4, quick start QS-bun

What ran: Bun getting-started reproduction on 2026-09-08, bun 1.4.2: quickstarts/bun, app-preload.log lines 1 and 101, on the plain documented --preload path.

DOC-Q5Node guide: the --require flag prints an undocumented "no longer supported" banner, and the ordering warning is stricter than developP2proven

Proposed issue docs-change

Update the Node getting-started page for --require and for first-line ESM imports

Description

platform-includes/getting-started-node/javascript.mdx:93-96 shows node --import ./instrument.mjs app.mjs, and :55 warns that importing the instrument file later than first means auto-instrumentation and modules like database monitoring may not work. Nothing on the page mentions --require.

Observed on develop with Node v26.5.0: node --require ./instrument.cjs app.mjs still delivers the same errors, spans, headers and logs, but its first stderr line is [Sentry] Initializing the SDK via the Node --require flag is no longer supported, because Node re-runs --require preloads on its module loader thread. Use --import instead, printed from packages/node/src/sdk/index.ts:126-133 whenever getEntryPointType() === 'require', unconditionally and not behind debug. A first-line import "./instrument.mjs" in the entry file gives the same telemetry as the flag, including Express route names and handler spans, because the injection hook is registered while @sentry/node evaluates. NODE_OPTIONS="--import ./instrument.mjs" is identical to the flag.

What to change:

  • Add one sentence that --require is no longer supported for initializing the SDK and prints a banner, with the --import replacement. Keep it narrow: common/install/late-initialization.mdx:34,37,106,132 recommends node --require @sentry/node/preload app.js, which no longer resolves on develop, because the preload entry point was removed from every server SDK (see DOC-Q26); the warning against --require can therefore stand without that exception.
  • Soften the ordering warning at :55: a first-line ESM import of the instrument file is equivalent to --import; the warning applies to imports that are not first.
  • Surface NODE_OPTIONS as an equivalent form on this page; it is already documented at common/install/esm.mdx:44-48.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-Q5, quick start QS-node

common/migration/v10-to-v11/index.mdx:695-707 already documents for upgraders that initializing via --require is no longer supported, so the remaining gap is the getting-started page, which new users reach first.

What ran: Node getting-started reproduction on 2026-09-08, Node v26.5.0: quickstarts/node, results-require-cjs.json, results-self-import.json, results-express-*.json. The banner is reproduced verbatim and every signal was still captured on the --require run.

DOC-Q85The Node install pages do not yet reflect the v11 decision on install methodsP1decision

Proposed issue docs-change

Restructure the Node install methods for v11: --import as the main path, bundler plus top-level import and --import @sentry/node/import as the alternatives, no CommonJS option

Description

The SDK team settled the v11 install methods for Node on 2026-09-09 while aligning the in-product onboarding (Linear SDK-1446); the docs pages do not reflect the decision yet, and the getting-started runs show why it matters:

  • node --import ./instrument.mjs stays the main documented path, because it guarantees the runtime wrapping and instruments as early as possible. Every Node quick start delivered the full telemetry on that path.
  • An alternative page documents the bundler plugin plus a top-level import, and explains the difference: a plain first-line import is not enough on its own because ESM hoists the framework imports ahead of Sentry.init. The runs measured that nuance: Express, Fastify, Hapi and Hono still got their spans and errors from a first-line import (their instrumentation registers at runtime), Koa lost every router and middleware span and, without the deprecated handler, its error capture (DOC-Q28, DOC-Q45). The pattern import "./instrument.mjs"; await import("./app.mjs"); makes a top-level import safe without a bundler.
  • An alternative page documents --import @sentry/node/import with a later Sentry.init(), replacing the late-initialization page that still rests on the removed @sentry/node/preload entry (DOC-Q26).
  • The CommonJS install option is dropped; the pages assume ESM.
  • Meta-frameworks are documented without --import, with an alternative that uses it and says when (for example when some packages are not bundled). Nuxt no longer emits the server config into the build, so it cannot be --imported at all (DOC-F11).

What to change:

  • Rework common/install/index.mdx, esm.mdx, esm-without-import.mdx, commonjs.mdx and late-initialization.mdx to the three methods above, record the decision on the pages rather than in a chat thread, and align the in-product onboarding snippets with it.

Source: runtime docs audit, item DOC-Q85, team decision of 2026-09-09 recorded for SDK-1446, cross-checked against the Node, Express, Fastify, Koa, Hapi and Hono quick starts

What ran: No run of its own; the item records the team decision and points at the quick starts that measured each method: quickstarts/node, express, fastify, koa, hapi and hono.
